# <img src={RasterXIcon} alt="RasterX" style={{height: '3em', width: 'auto', verticalAlign: 'middle', marginRight: '0.5rem'}} /> Function Reference

RasterX functions run in two execution tiers — **lightweight** (pure-Python `pyrx`) and **heavyweight** (`rasterx`). As of v0.4.0, **every RasterX function is available in both tiers** (badged `<Tier both/>` per function below); per-function notes call out where the lightweight implementation differs. See [Choosing an Execution Tier](./execution-tiers) for the comparison and the lightweight install.

Complete reference for all RasterX functions with detailed descriptions, parameters, return values, and examples.

:::tip Built on rasterio
RasterX's lightweight tier is, in large part, **distributed rasterio** + a best-of-breed raster stack. See [Rasterio Distributed](./rasterio-distributed) for the coverage matrix and honest gaps.
:::

## Overview

RasterX is GeoBrix's raster data processing package, providing comprehensive tools for working with raster datasets such as satellite imagery, elevation models, and other gridded spatial data. It is a refactor and improvement of Mosaic raster functions, extended through v0.5.0 with terrain analysis, spectral indices, vector-raster bridging, web-mercator tile output, H3/quadbin/BNG grid aggregations, and — in v0.5.0 — **virtual tiles**, a **COG-preparation lane**, and **VRT mosaics** for memory-safe processing of multi-gigabyte rasters. Since the Databricks product does not (yet) support anything built-in specifically for raster processing, RasterX provides a gap-filling capability for raster operations on the Databricks platform.

## Key Features

- **GDAL-Powered**: Leverages GDAL for robust raster format support
- **Distributed Processing**: Built on Spark for scalable raster operations
- **Multiple Format Support**: GeoTIFF, COG, NetCDF, and other GDAL-supported formats
- **Metadata Extraction**: Comprehensive raster metadata access
- **Raster Operations**: Clipping, resampling, transformations, map algebra
- **Band Operations**: Multi-band raster support, single-band extraction
- **Terrain Analysis**: Slope, aspect, hillshade, TRI, TPI, roughness, color-relief
- **Spectral Indices**: EVI, SAVI, NDWI, NBR, NDVI, plus a generic dispatcher
- **Vector-Raster Bridge**: Rasterize geometries, polygonize value regions
- **Tile Publishing**: Web-mercator XYZ tile generation (PNG / JPEG / WebP)
- **Grid Aggregations**: H3, CARTO quadbin v0, and BNG cell aggregations
- **Virtual Tiles**: Bytes-free windowed reads — process multi-gigabyte rasters without materializing them into executor memory
- **COG Preparation & VRT Mosaics**: `file_gbx` + `cog_gbx` prepare spec-valid Cloud-Optimized GeoTIFFs and portable VRT mosaics for cheap windowed reads

:::note Coordinate reference systems
Many RasterX functions accept CRS parameters. Use `srid` / `out_srid` (integer EPSG or ESRI code) or `crs` / `out_crs` (CRS string — EPSG, ESRI, OGC, WKT, PROJ4) to declare or project CRS. Functions that consume a clip polygon or sample geometry accept `clip_crs` / `crs` as a source-CRS fallback. `rst_srid` returns the stored SRID integer; `rst_crs` returns the canonical CRS string and always returns a value. See [Coordinate reference systems](./coordinate-reference-systems) for the full model, the EPSG→ESRI resolution order, and the ESRI/OGC authority-preservation behavior.
:::

## Function Categories

RasterX exposes 129 SQL functions (registered as `gbx_rst_*`; available in Python and Scala as `rst_*`), organized into the following categories (see [rasterx/functions.scala](https://github.com/databrickslabs/geobrix/blob/main/src/main/scala/com/databricks/labs/gbx/rasterx/functions.scala)):

![RasterX function categories — Constructors, Accessors, Aggregators, Generators, Operations, H3 Grid](../../../resources/images/diagrams/rasterx/rasterx-function-categories.png)

- **Accessor Functions**: Read raster properties and metadata (bounds, dimensions, CRS, bands, pixel size, georeference, format, type, NoData, subdatasets, summary, etc.)
- **Aggregator Functions**: Combine or merge rasters in group-by (combineavg_agg, derivedband_agg, merge_agg, h3_rasterize_agg)
- **Constructor Functions**: Create or load rasters from paths, binary content, or bands
- **Generator Functions**: Produce multiple tiles or bands (h3_tessellate, maketiles, retile, separatebands, tooverlappingtiles)
- **Grid Functions (H3)**: Aggregate raster values to H3 cells (rastertogrid avg/count/max/min/median/sum/variance/stddev)
- **Grid Functions (quadbin)**: Aggregate raster values to CARTO quadbin v0 cells (rastertogrid avg/count/max/min/median/sum/variance/stddev; tessellate; rasterize_agg)
- **Grid Functions (BNG)**: Aggregate raster values to British National Grid cells (rastertogrid avg/count/max/min/median/sum/variance/stddev; tessellate; rasterize_agg)
- **Operations**: Transform and analyze rasters (clip, transform, merge, asformat, ndvi, filter, convolve, map algebra, coordinate conversion, isEmpty, tryOpen, initNoData, updateType, combineavg, derivedband)
- **Web-Mercator Tile Output**: Reproject to EPSG:3857 and emit slippy-map XYZ tiles (to_webmercator, tilexyz, xyzpyramid)
- **Vector-raster bridge**: Burn polygons into rasters and trace contiguous regions back to polygons (rasterize, polygonize)
- **Terrain Analysis**: DEM-derived surfaces from `gdal.DEMProcessing` (slope, aspect, hillshade, TRI, TPI, roughness, color relief)
- **Spectral Indices**: Multi-band satellite math (EVI, SAVI, NDWI, NBR, plus the generic `rst_index` dispatcher)

## Tile payload

Every RasterX (heavyweight) function returns a **materialized** tile whose `raster` field is a **self-contained, in-memory raster** (GTiff by default) — safe to serialize between Spark stages and executors, persist to Delta, hand off to `rasterio` / `gdal`, or write back out via the `gdal` writer. The bytes are never an XML reference to a per-executor `/vsimem/` tempfile or to a path that only exists on the producing node.

See **[Tile structure](./tile-structure)** for the full tile-struct schema (the shared `cellid` / `raster` / `path` / `window` / … struct), and **[Virtual Tiles](./virtual-tiles)** for the bytes-free lightweight-tier variant and how tiles move between materialized and virtual.

Functions that internally build via an intermediate VRT — `gbx_rst_merge`, `gbx_rst_merge_agg`, `gbx_rst_frombands`, `gbx_rst_combineavg`, `gbx_rst_combineavg_agg`, `gbx_rst_derivedband`, `gbx_rst_derivedband_agg` — materialize the result to GTiff before returning, so downstream stages on different executors see real raster bytes. Inspect a tile's payload format from `tile.metadata.driver`; for any of the functions above, it will read `GTiff` (not `VRT`). See [Release Notes](../release-notes-0-5-and-earlier#whats-new-in-v030) for the v0.3.0 correctness fix that introduced this invariant. See [Tile structure](./tile-structure) for the full tile-struct schema.

## Setup

With the geobrix library already installed ([Installation](../installation)), pick your execution tier and run this once. Both tiers alias the module as `rx`, so every example below is identical regardless of tier — only this setup differs. (See [Choosing an Execution Tier](./execution-tiers) for the comparison.)

The examples on this page read from **four temp views**, each a raster loaded as a `tile` column. Point the four path placeholders at your own rasters:

| Placeholder | Sample file | Reader (light&nbsp;/&nbsp;heavy) — Temp view | Backs |
|---|---|---|---|
| `GTIFF_SAMPLE_DIR` | single-band GeoTIFF | `gtiff_gbx` / `gtiff_gdal` — `rasters` | **Default** — most accessor, tile-ops, transform, and generator examples |
| `GTIFF_MULTI_DIR` | multi-band GeoTIFF (red/NIR/green) | `gtiff_gbx` / `gtiff_gdal` — `multiband_rasters` | band-math and spectral-index examples, `rst_numbands`, `rst_bandmetadata` |
| `DTM_DIR` | digital elevation model | `gtiff_gbx` / `gtiff_gdal` — `dem_rasters` | terrain examples (`rst_slope`, `rst_aspect`, …) |
| `NETCDF_DIR` | NetCDF with subdatasets | `netcdf_gbx` / `netcdf_gdal` — `netcdf_rasters` | `rst_subdatasets`, `rst_getsubdataset` |

Each format has a named reader in **both** tiers (light `*_gbx` / heavy `*_gdal`); the setup for each tier below uses its own. The generic readers (`raster_gbx` light, `gdal` heavy) also read any raster their tier's engine supports.

The four `*_DIR` placeholders in the code below are wired to small sample rasters **committed in the GeoBrix repo** under [`src/test/resources/binary/`](https://github.com/databrickslabs/geobrix/tree/main/src/test/resources/binary) (resolved by the `_SAMPLE_PATHS[...]` / `*_path()` helpers you'll see in the snippet). To run the examples yourself, stage those sample files — or your own rasters — in a Unity Catalog Volume directory and set each placeholder to its Volume path.

The setup below loads `GTIFF_SAMPLE_DIR` into `rasters` and then loads the other three the same way — swap the path and view name (and, for NetCDF, the reader). Every example on the page assumes these four views exist.

Registration is only needed for the SQL functions — the Scala `rx.*` Column API works without it.

## Tier availability

As of v0.4.0, **all RasterX functions run in both execution tiers** — the lightweight `pyrx` (pure-Python) and heavyweight `rasterx` tiers share the same `rst_*` / `gbx_rst_*` names, and each function below carries a `:::note Lightweight tier (pyrx)` admonition with its backing library and any behavioral differences. For the heavyweight VRT Python pixel-function configuration (used by `gbx_rst_combineavg` / `gbx_rst_derivedband`), see **[VRT Python pixel functions](#vrt-python-pixel-functions)** at the end of this page.

## Examples — Conventions

Every function on this page shows one example, expressed identically across four tabs. This section defines the shared setup so each function tab contains only its invocation — nothing more.

### Canonical sample files

Each function uses one of four canonical files, chosen by what the function demonstrates:

| File | What it demonstrates | Used by |
|---|---|---|
| `nyc_sentinel2_red.tif` | Single-band GeoTIFF (Sentinel-2 red band, NYC area) | **Default** — most accessor, tile-ops, transform, and generator functions |
| `rgb_nir_small.tif` | 3-band GeoTIFF with per-band metadata (red, NIR, green — 8×8 px) | Band-math and spectral-index functions (`rst_ndvi`, `rst_evi`, …), `rst_numbands`, `rst_bandmetadata` |
| `srtm_n40w073.tif` | Digital Elevation Model (SRTM, NYC area) | Terrain functions (`rst_slope`, `rst_aspect`, `rst_hillshade`, …) |
| CMIP5 NetCDF (`prAdjust_day_…nc`) | Multi-variable NetCDF with two subdatasets | `rst_subdatasets`, `rst_getsubdataset` only |

### The `tile`-column convention

In every example on this page:

- **SQL:** `rasters` is a temporary view whose `tile` column holds the canonical sample loaded via the reader. `FROM rasters` means "the sample as tiles."
- **Python (light and heavy):** `df` is a DataFrame with a `tile` column loaded from the canonical file. `df.select(...)` means "apply this function to the sample tiles."
- **Scala:** `rasters` is a DataFrame with the same `tile` column. `rasters.select(...)` is identical in intent to the Python form.

Each function's example therefore shows only the invocation — the load is the convention, not the code.

### How to read the four tabs

| Tab | Tier | Color |
|---|---|---|
| **SQL** | Both (default) | — |
| **Python (light)** | `pyrx` lightweight tier | — |
| **Python (heavy)** | `rasterx` heavyweight tier | Blue badge |
| **Scala** | `rasterx` heavyweight tier | Blue badge |

All four tabs show the **same operation** on the **same file**. Where a tier's output genuinely differs in form, a short note explains why — for example:

- Geometry-returning functions (`rst_boundingbox`, `rst_georeference`): SQL returns WKT; Python and Scala return a binary column. The note reads `...` `(WKB binary)`.
- Subdataset maps (`rst_subdatasets`): SQL returns `map<string,string>`; rendered as `{SUBDATASET_1_NAME -> ..., SUBDATASET_1_DESC -> ...}`. A note clarifies the key pattern.
- Band-metadata maps (`rst_bandmetadata`): similarly noted where the rendering differs between tiers.

Scalar values are **identical across all tabs** — same fixture, same function, same result.

Tile-returning functions (`rst_clip`, `rst_resample`, `rst_transform`, the aggregators, the constructors, …) return a **[v2 Tile](./tile-structure)**. Their output is shown as a representative struct — `{0, <raster bytes>, <virtual path>, {driver -> GTiff, ...}}` — rather than a literal byte dump. Tiles loaded via `rst_fromcontent` / `rst_fromfile` (as in [Setup](#setup)) are **materialized**: the `raster` field holds the encoded bytes and `path` is null. [**Virtual tiles**](./virtual-tiles) — a populated `path` with lazily-read bytes — arise from the force-output parameters, not from the default load.

**SQL naming and arguments:** in the SQL tab, RasterX functions carry the **`gbx_`** prefix (e.g. `gbx_rst_boundingbox`, `gbx_rst_width`); Python and Scala use the bare `rst_*` name via `rx`. In the lightweight tier the registered `gbx_rst_*` SQL functions require **every argument to be passed explicitly** — optional defaults are honored only through the Python `rx.*` API. See [Language Bindings](./language-bindings) for more.

### Per-function notes

Functions that use a non-default fixture (multiband GeoTIFF, DEM, or NetCDF) carry a one-line note immediately before their code example flagging the file. Functions that produce a tile rather than consuming one (constructors: `rst_fromfile`, `rst_fromcontent`, `rst_frombands`, …) show a fuller load/build example with a note — the bare-invocation model does not apply when there is no input `tile` yet.

### rst_crs

{/* BEGIN GENERATED gbx_rst_crs: edit manifest/functions.json, then run gbx:manifest:gen */}
<Surfaces session python scala />

| Surface | Call | Returns |
|---|---|---|
| SQL (session) | `gbx_rst_crs(tile)` | `STRING` |
| Python | `rasterx.functions.rst_crs(tile)` | Column |

| Parameter | SQL type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `tile` | `TILE` |  | The raster tile. |
{/* END GENERATED gbx_rst_crs */}

:::note Lightweight tier (pyrx)
Powered by **rasterio** + **pyproj**.
:::

The CRS of the tile as a string: `EPSG:4326`, `ESRI:54008` or, with no authority code, its WKT. Every raster with a CRS gets a value, including an authority-less one that `rst_srid` cannot name. A raster with no CRS gives `NULL`. See [Coordinate Reference Systems](./coordinate-reference-systems).

Returns the authority string (`AUTHORITY:CODE`, e.g. `EPSG:4326` or `ESRI:54008`) when the CRS has one, otherwise its WKT. `rst_srid` gives the integer authority code, and `NULL` when the CRS has none. `rst_crs` names every CRS a raster has, including an authority-less one defined by WKT or PROJ4.

:::tip CRS: SRID int vs CRS string
- **`rst_srid`** returns the integer authority code, EPSG or ESRI (for example `4326`, or `54008` for `ESRI:54008`). It returns `NULL` for a raster with no CRS, an authority-less WKT or PROJ4 CRS, or a code that is not a number (such as `OGC:CRS84`). Use it when you need the numeric SRID for the native ST bridge.
- **`rst_crs`** returns the CRS string and never loses a non-EPSG CRS.
- **`rst_setcrs`** / **`rst_transformcrs`** take a CRS *string*. An int-castable string is treated as an EPSG SRID (`'4326'` behaves like SRID `4326`); otherwise the string is parsed as an authority code (`EPSG:`/`ESRI:`), WKT, or PROJ4. This is how ESRI codes, WKT, and PROJ4 definitions survive a round trip.
:::

### rst_georeference

{/* BEGIN GENERATED gbx_rst_georeference: edit manifest/functions.json, then run gbx:manifest:gen */}
<Surfaces session python scala />

| Surface | Call | Returns |
|---|---|---|
| SQL (session) | `gbx_rst_georeference(tile)` | `map<string,double>` |
| Python | `rasterx.functions.rst_georeference(tile)` | Column |

| Parameter | SQL type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `tile` | `TILE` |  | The raster tile. |
{/* END GENERATED gbx_rst_georeference */}

:::note Lightweight tier (pyrx)
Powered by **rasterio**.
:::

Georeference parameters as a map.

The result is a **MapType** with the following keys, corresponding to [GDAL's 6-element geotransform](https://gdal.org/en/stable/tutorials/geotransforms_tut.html):

| Key | Geotransform index | Meaning |
|-----|--------------------|---------|
| `upperLeftX` | GT(0) | X of the upper-left corner of the upper-left pixel |
| `upperLeftY` | GT(3) | Y of the upper-left corner of the upper-left pixel |
| `scaleX` | GT(1) | Pixel width (west–east resolution) |
| `scaleY` | GT(5) | Pixel height (north–south resolution; often negative for north-up) |
| `skewX` | GT(2) | Row rotation (typically 0) |
| `skewY` | GT(4) | Column rotation (typically 0) |

See the [GDAL geotransform tutorial](https://gdal.org/en/stable/tutorials/geotransforms_tut.html) and [raster data model](https://gdal.org/en/stable/user/raster_data_model.html) for details.

### rst_getsubdataset

{/* BEGIN GENERATED gbx_rst_getsubdataset: edit manifest/functions.json, then run gbx:manifest:gen */}
<Surfaces session python scala />

| Surface | Call | Returns |
|---|---|---|
| SQL (session) | `gbx_rst_getsubdataset(tile, subset_name)` | `TILE` |
| Python | `rasterx.functions.rst_getsubdataset(tile, subset_name)` | Column |

| Parameter | SQL type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `tile` | `TILE` |  | A tile whose raster is a container with subdatasets, such as a NetCDF file. |
| `subset_name` | `STRING` |  | The name of the subdataset (the last part of its GDAL name, such as `'prAdjust'`). A name that matches none raises. |
{/* END GENERATED gbx_rst_getsubdataset */}

:::note Lightweight tier (pyrx)
Powered by **rasterio**. Subdataset availability depends on rasterio's bundled GDAL driver set.
:::

Extract a subdataset of a container file. A tile that holds no subdataset of that name raises an error that names it.

_Examples use the CMIP5 NetCDF fixture (`prAdjust_day_HadGEM2-CC_*.nc`) which has two subdatasets: `time_bnds` and `prAdjust`. Subdatasets require a multi-layer format such as NetCDF; plain GeoTIFFs return no subdatasets._

  testFile="docs/tests/python/api/test_rasterx_functions_sql.py"
/>

## Aggregator Functions

Combine or merge rasters in group-by (7 total).

### rst_bng_rasterize_agg

{/* BEGIN GENERATED gbx_rst_bng_rasterize_agg: edit manifest/functions.json, then run gbx:manifest:gen */}
<Surfaces session python scala />

| Surface | Call | Returns |
|---|---|---|
| SQL (session) | `gbx_rst_bng_rasterize_agg(cellid, value, out_srid, pixel_size, xmin, ymin, xmax, ymax, width, height, mode, kring_pad, [out_crs])` | `BINARY` |
| Python | `rasterx.functions.rst_bng_rasterize_agg(cellid, value, out_srid, pixel_size, xmin, ymin, xmax, ymax, width, height, mode, kring_pad, out_crs=None)` | Column |

| Parameter | SQL type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `cellid` | `STRING` |  | The BNG cell id, for example `'TQ3080'`. |
| `value` | `DOUBLE` |  | The value burned for the cell. A repeated cell burns its largest value; a NULL value in a typed column never wins against a number, and a cell of nothing but NULL burns NaN. An untyped NULL (the argument left out, or `None` in Python) is the presence mask and burns 1.0. |
| `out_srid` | `INT` |  | Not read: the output is always EPSG:27700. |
| `pixel_size` | `DOUBLE` |  | The pixel size in metres. |
| `xmin` | `DOUBLE` |  | The west edge of the canvas in EPSG:27700 metres. |
| `ymin` | `DOUBLE` |  | The south edge of the canvas in EPSG:27700 metres. |
| `xmax` | `DOUBLE` |  | The east edge of the canvas in EPSG:27700 metres. |
| `ymax` | `DOUBLE` |  | The north edge of the canvas in EPSG:27700 metres. |
| `width` | `INT` |  | The canvas width in pixels. |
| `height` | `INT` |  | The canvas height in pixels. |
| `mode` | `STRING` |  | How the canvas is derived when no extent is given, for example `'centroids'`. |
| `kring_pad` | `INT` |  | Cells of padding around the derived canvas. |
| `out_crs` | `STRING` | `NULL` | Must be NULL. Reproject the tile with `gbx_rst_transformcrs` instead. |
{/* END GENERATED gbx_rst_bng_rasterize_agg */}

<Impl groupedAgg/>

If a cell id repeats in a group with different values, the **largest** value is burned, whatever order the rows arrive in. In the lightweight tier a null value (or `NaN`) never wins against a number. A cell whose values are all null burns `NaN`, also when the whole `value` column is null. Only an omitted `value` (or an untyped `NULL`) is the presence mask and burns `1.0`. A `DECIMAL` column reaches the aggregate without its type, so a column of `DECIMAL` nulls counts as omitted too: cast it to `DOUBLE` first. A `NaN` that is a real value, not a null, is ignored the same way on both tiers. Beside a number in one cell, the number is burned. A cell whose values are all `NaN` burns `NaN`.

:::note Lightweight tier (pyrx)
The lightweight implementation is backed by `pygx._bng` cell math and `rasterio`. BNG cell IDs (**STRING**) are parsed via `pygx._bng` to EPSG:27700-native cell geometries and burned into the output band — the output canvas is EPSG:27700 throughout with no warp step (this aggregator takes cellid+value rows, not a raster tile).
:::

:::warning NoData sentinel is -9999.0
Pixels not covered by any geometry in the group are set to **`-9999.0`** (band-registered NoData). Filter or mask downstream via `gbx_rst_getnodata` or `IS NULL` on the extracted band value.
:::

:::warning `gbx_rst_bng_rasterize_agg` returns BINARY
In SQL and Python the aggregate returns the raster as `BINARY` (the GeoTIFF bytes), not a tile. A grouped aggregate cannot return the tile struct there. The Scala function `rst_bng_rasterize_agg` keeps returning the tile struct. Wrap the SQL or Python result with `gbx_rst_fromcontent` to get a tile back:

```sql
SELECT
  cell_id AS cellid,
  gbx_rst_fromcontent(gbx_rst_bng_rasterize_agg(cellid, burn_value), 'GTiff') AS tile
FROM bng_cell_values
GROUP BY cell_id
```

The Python call `rx.rst_bng_rasterize_agg(...)` returns the same `BINARY` column. Pass it to `rx.rst_fromcontent(col, "GTiff")` for a tile.
:::

Streaming aggregator that burns BNG cell geometry/value pairs (one row per cell) into a single rasterized tile per group. The output tile is always in EPSG:27700 (British National Grid). BNG cell IDs are **STRING**. The inverse of `rst_bng_rastertogrid*`: where those functions reduce raster pixels to per-cell statistics, this one synthesizes a raster from per-cell values.

:::note Optional trailing `out_crs` parameter (refused when non-NULL, on both tiers)
Both tiers accept an optional trailing `out_crs` (string CRS) argument so the signature matches on each, but a non-NULL value is refused with a parameter error on either tier. The output is always EPSG:27700: pass `NULL` for `out_crs`, and reproject the tile with `rst_transformcrs` when another CRS is wanted. `out_srid` is not read on either tier (it exists for signature parity with the H3/quadbin aggregators).
:::

_Multi-row fixture: 3 BNG 1km STRING cell rows near central London (EPSG:27700) with burn values 1.0/2.0/3.0. All tabs use the same grouped-agg rasterize invocation._

  python={rasterxAggregatorsLightCode} pythonSource="docs/tests/python/api/rasterx_aggregators_python_light.py"
  scala={scalaApiExamplesCode}          scalaSource="docs/tests/scala/api/ScalaApiExamples.scala"

### rst_bng_rasterize_tvf

{/* BEGIN GENERATED gbx_rst_bng_rasterize_tvf: edit manifest/functions.json, then run gbx:manifest:gen */}
<Surfaces session python />

| Surface | Call | Returns |
|---|---|---|
| SQL (session) | `gbx_rst_bng_rasterize_tvf(rows, xmin, ymin, xmax, ymax, width, height)` | rows |
| Python | `rasterx.functions.rst_bng_rasterize_tvf(df, xmin, ymin, xmax, ymax, width, height)` | DataFrame |

Rows: `key` `STRING`, `raster` `BINARY`.

| Parameter | SQL type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `rows` | `TABLE` |  | The cells, as `TABLE(SELECT key, cellid, value FROM ...) PARTITION BY (key)`: one canvas per key. `cellid` is the BNG cell id (for example `'TQ3080'`) and `value` the value burned for it. A repeated cell burns its largest value; a NULL value never wins against a number, and a cell of nothing but NULL burns NaN. A NULL cell id is skipped. A partition that holds more than one key raises. A NULL canvas value raises and names the value. |
| `xmin` | `DOUBLE` |  | The west edge of the canvas in EPSG:27700 metres. |
| `ymin` | `DOUBLE` |  | The south edge of the canvas in EPSG:27700 metres. |
| `xmax` | `DOUBLE` |  | The east edge of the canvas in EPSG:27700 metres. |
| `ymax` | `DOUBLE` |  | The north edge of the canvas in EPSG:27700 metres. |
| `width` | `INT` |  | The canvas width in pixels. |
| `height` | `INT` |  | The canvas height in pixels. |
{/* END GENERATED gbx_rst_bng_rasterize_tvf */}

Use it instead of [`rst_bng_rasterize_agg`](#rst_bng_rasterize_agg) for groups of many cells: `SELECT * FROM gbx_rst_bng_rasterize_tvf(TABLE(SELECT key, cellid, value FROM cells) PARTITION BY (key), xmin, ymin, xmax, ymax, width, height)`. It reads each key's cells a row at a time and keeps only the largest value per cell. It needs less memory than the grouped aggregate and burns the same pixels. The canvas is explicit, and a key with no cell gives no row.

{/* BEGIN GENERATED gbx_rst_bng_rasterize: edit manifest/functions.json, then run gbx:manifest:gen */}

| Surface | Call | Returns |
|---|---|---|
| SQL (session) | `gbx_rst_bng_rasterize(cells, out_srid, pixel_size, xmin, ymin, xmax, ymax, width, height, mode, kring_pad, [out_crs])` | `BINARY` |
| Python | `rasterx.functions.rst_bng_rasterize(cells, out_srid, pixel_size, xmin, ymin, xmax, ymax, width, height, mode, kring_pad, out_crs=None)` | Column |

| Parameter | SQL type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `cells` | `array<struct<cellid:string,value:double>>` |  | The group's cells as an array of structs `(cellid, value)`, for example `collect_list(struct(cellid, value))`. The rules of the aggregate hold: a repeated cell burns its largest value, a NULL cell id is skipped. |
| `out_srid` | `INT` |  | Not read: the output is always EPSG:27700. |
| `pixel_size` | `DOUBLE` |  | The pixel size in metres. |
| `xmin` | `DOUBLE` |  | The west edge of the canvas in EPSG:27700 metres. |
| `ymin` | `DOUBLE` |  | The south edge of the canvas in EPSG:27700 metres. |
| `xmax` | `DOUBLE` |  | The east edge of the canvas in EPSG:27700 metres. |
| `ymax` | `DOUBLE` |  | The north edge of the canvas in EPSG:27700 metres. |
| `width` | `INT` |  | The canvas width in pixels. |
| `height` | `INT` |  | The canvas height in pixels. |
| `mode` | `STRING` |  | How the canvas is derived when no extent is given, for example `'centroids'`. |
| `kring_pad` | `INT` |  | Cells of padding around the derived canvas. |
| `out_crs` | `STRING` | `NULL` | Must be NULL. Reproject the tile with `gbx_rst_transformcrs` instead. |
{/* END GENERATED gbx_rst_bng_rasterize */}

Rasterize an array of BNG cells into one tile, the scalar twin of [`rst_bng_rasterize_agg`](#rst_bng_rasterize_agg). Collect the group's cells first, for example `gbx_rst_bng_rasterize(collect_list(struct(cellid, value)), ...)`. The answer is the same bytes as the aggregate gives. Use it for a group the aggregate's streaming shape cannot take, such as a Serverless finalize or a group from an earlier step. It is a session function, with no Unity Catalog form.

### rst_dtmfromgeoms_agg

{/* BEGIN GENERATED gbx_rst_dtmfromgeoms_agg: edit manifest/functions.json, then run gbx:manifest:gen */}
<Surfaces session python />

| Surface | Call | Returns |
|---|---|---|
| SQL (session) | `gbx_rst_dtmfromgeoms_agg(point, breaklines, merge_tolerance, snap_tolerance, xmin, ymin, xmax, ymax, width_px, height_px, [out_srid], [no_data], [out_crs])` | `BINARY` |
| Python | `rasterx.functions.rst_dtmfromgeoms_agg(point, breaklines, merge_tolerance, snap_tolerance, xmin, ymin, xmax, ymax, width_px, height_px, out_srid=None, no_data=None, out_crs=None)` | Column |

| Parameter | SQL type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `point` | `BINARY` or `STRING` |  | A point with a Z coordinate: WKB, EWKB, WKT or EWKT. An empty one is skipped. |
| `breaklines` | `ARRAY<BINARY>` or `ARRAY<STRING>` |  | Breaklines, the same for the whole group. Accepted but not enforced. |
| `merge_tolerance` | `DOUBLE` |  | Accepted, not applied. |
| `snap_tolerance` | `DOUBLE` |  | Accepted, not applied. |
| `xmin` | `DOUBLE` |  | The west edge of the output. |
| `ymin` | `DOUBLE` |  | The south edge. |
| `xmax` | `DOUBLE` |  | The east edge. |
| `ymax` | `DOUBLE` |  | The north edge. |
| `width_px` | `INT` |  | The output width in pixels. |
| `height_px` | `INT` |  | The output height in pixels. |
| `out_srid` | `INT` | `NULL` | The EPSG or ESRI code of the output CRS. NULL leaves the output without a CRS. |
| `no_data` | `DOUBLE` | `-9999.0` | The NoData value. |
| `out_crs` | `STRING` | `NULL` | The output CRS as `EPSG:<n>`, `ESRI:<n>` or WKT. When set it wins over `out_srid`; setting both raises. |
{/* END GENERATED gbx_rst_dtmfromgeoms_agg */}

<Impl groupedAgg/>

:::note Lightweight tier (pyrx)
Powered by **rasterio** + **SciPy** (`scipy.spatial.Delaunay`). Aggregate — builds one TIN DTM tile per group from the group's Z-valued points via barycentric interpolation over an **unconstrained** Delaunay triangulation; `breaklines`, `merge_tolerance`, and `snap_tolerance` are accepted but not enforced (the heavyweight tier builds a constrained TIN). The lightweight Python binding also accepts an optional trailing `out_crs` (string CRS) argument that the SQL/heavyweight tiers do not — the heavyweight builder takes the int `out_srid` only. `out_crs` wins over `out_srid` when both are given. This is a lightweight superset, not a heavyweight regression.
:::

:::note The aggregate returns BINARY
In SQL and Python the aggregate returns the raster as `BINARY` (the GeoTIFF bytes), on every compute. Wrap it with `gbx_rst_fromcontent(<aggregate>, 'GTiff')` to get a tile back.
:::

Streaming aggregator that accepts one Z-valued point WKB per row and produces a TIN/Delaunay DTM raster tile per group; breaklines are supplied as a per-group constant array to enforce hard terrain edges.

_Multi-row fixture: 4 Z-valued WKB POINT rows (elevation 100–250 m) over a `[0,0,1,1]` EPSG:4326 extent. All tabs use the same grouped-agg invocation._

<FunctionExamples
  name="rst_dtmfromgeoms_agg"
  sql={rasterxSqlCode}                  sqlSource="docs/tests/python/api/rasterx_functions_sql.py"
  python={rasterxAggregatorsLightCode} pythonSource="docs/tests/python/api/rasterx_aggregators_python_light.py"
  testFile="docs/tests/python/api/test_rasterx_aggregators_python_light.py"
/>

:::note Memory & scale — thin the points that feed a TIN

A Delaunay TIN must hold **all** of a group's points in memory to triangulate, so peak memory is
**proportional to the points per group** — and, unlike [`rst_binpoints_agg`](#rst_binpoints_agg),
a triangulation is inherently global and **cannot** be made grid-bounded the way
[`bin_points_tiled`](#rst_binpoints_agg) bounds binning. At full LiDAR density a single tile's ground
returns (hundreds of thousands to millions of points) can exhaust worker RAM.

A bare-earth TIN is near-identical from a **representative subset**, so thin the input points before
the aggregate — `df.sample(fraction=…)` down to a bounded per-group count (e.g. ~150 k), or use
smaller tiles. This is standard LiDAR TIN practice and keeps memory bounded regardless of source
density. See the [LiDAR reader](../readers/lidar) and [Performance](./performance).

:::

| `max_pts` | `INT` | `12` | The number of nearest points used per pixel, above 0. |
| `out_crs` | `STRING` | `NULL` | The output CRS as `EPSG:<n>`, `ESRI:<n>` or WKT. When set it wins over `out_srid`; setting both raises. |
{/* END GENERATED gbx_rst_gridfrompoints_agg */}

<Impl groupedAgg/>

:::note Lightweight tier (pyrx)
Powered by **rasterio** + **SciPy** (`cKDTree` IDW). Aggregate — `groupBy(...).agg(rx.rst_gridfrompoints_agg(...))` inverse-distance-interpolates the group's points into one Float64 grid tile (NoData −9999). The lightweight Python binding also accepts an optional trailing `out_crs` (string CRS) argument that the SQL/heavyweight tiers do not — the heavyweight builder takes the int `out_srid` only. `out_crs` wins over `out_srid` when both are given. This is a lightweight superset, not a heavyweight regression.
:::

:::note The aggregate returns BINARY
In SQL and Python the aggregate returns the raster as `BINARY` (the GeoTIFF bytes), on every compute. Wrap it with `gbx_rst_fromcontent(<aggregate>, 'GTiff')` to get a tile back.
:::

Streaming IDW-interpolation aggregator that accepts one point geometry and one scalar value per row and produces a Float64 GeoTIFF tile per group; use when observations arrive one per row rather than as pre-built arrays.

_Multi-row fixture: 4 WKB POINT rows with observations 10–40 over a `[0,0,1,1]` EPSG:4326 extent. All tabs use the same grouped-agg IDW invocation._

### rst_h3_rasterize_agg

{/* BEGIN GENERATED gbx_rst_h3_rasterize_agg: edit manifest/functions.json, then run gbx:manifest:gen */}
<Surfaces session python scala />

| Surface | Call | Returns |
|---|---|---|
| SQL (session) | `gbx_rst_h3_rasterize_agg(cellid, value, out_srid, pixel_size, xmin, ymin, xmax, ymax, width, height, mode, kring_pad, [out_crs])` | `BINARY` |
| Python | `rasterx.functions.rst_h3_rasterize_agg(cellid, value, out_srid, pixel_size, xmin, ymin, xmax, ymax, width, height, mode, kring_pad, out_crs=None)` | Column |

| Parameter | SQL type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `cellid` | `BIGINT` |  | The H3 cell id. |
| `value` | `DOUBLE` |  | The value burned for the cell. A repeated cell burns its largest value; a NULL value in a typed column never wins against a number, and a cell of nothing but NULL burns NaN. An untyped NULL (the argument left out, or `None` in Python) is the presence mask and burns 1.0. |
| `out_srid` | `INT` |  | The EPSG code of the output CRS. |
| `pixel_size` | `DOUBLE` |  | The pixel size in CRS units; NULL takes it from the cells' resolution. |
| `xmin` | `DOUBLE` |  | The west edge of the canvas; NULL derives the canvas from the cells. |
| `ymin` | `DOUBLE` |  | The south edge of the canvas. |
| `xmax` | `DOUBLE` |  | The east edge of the canvas. |
| `ymax` | `DOUBLE` |  | The north edge of the canvas. |
| `width` | `INT` |  | The canvas width in pixels. |
| `height` | `INT` |  | The canvas height in pixels. |
| `mode` | `STRING` |  | How the canvas is derived when no extent is given: `'centroids'` or `'spatial_envelope'`. |
| `kring_pad` | `INT` |  | Rings of padding around the derived canvas. |
| `out_crs` | `STRING` | `NULL` | The output CRS as `EPSG:<n>`; when set it wins over `out_srid`. |
{/* END GENERATED gbx_rst_h3_rasterize_agg */}

<Impl groupedAgg/>

If a cell id repeats in a group with different values, the **largest** value is burned, whatever order the rows arrive in. In the lightweight tier a null value (or `NaN`) never wins against a number. A cell whose values are all null burns `NaN`, also when the whole `value` column is null. Only an omitted `value` (or an untyped `NULL`) is the presence mask and burns `1.0`. A `DECIMAL` column reaches the aggregate without its type, so a column of `DECIMAL` nulls counts as omitted too: cast it to `DOUBLE` first.

:::note Lightweight tier (pyrx)
Powered by **rasterio** + **h3**. Aggregate — `groupBy(...).agg(rx.rst_h3_rasterize_agg("cellid", "value", ...))` burns each H3 cell's centroid pixel (or spatial-envelope pixels with `mode='spatial_envelope'`) into one raster tile per group. When `value` is omitted (or an untyped `NULL`), all burned pixels carry `1.0` (presence mask); a typed column of nulls burns `NaN` in the lightweight tier. The extent and pixel size are derived automatically from the H3 resolution unless explicit bounds are supplied; `kring_pad` (default 1) expands the canvas by that many rings to avoid clipping edge cells.
:::

:::note The aggregate returns BINARY
In SQL and Python the aggregate returns the raster as `BINARY` (the GeoTIFF bytes), on every compute. Wrap it with `gbx_rst_fromcontent(<aggregate>, 'GTiff')` to get a tile back.
:::

Streaming aggregator that burns H3 cell centroid pixels (or spatial-envelope pixels) into one raster tile per group. This is the **inverse** of [`rst_h3_rastertogrid*`](#rst_h3_rastertogridavg): where those functions reduce raster pixels to per-cell statistics, `rst_h3_rasterize_agg` reconstructs a raster from per-cell values. Use [`rst_frombands_agg`](#rst_frombands_agg) to stack per-threshold rasters (each produced by one `rst_h3_rasterize_agg` call) into a single multi-band output.

:::note Lightweight-only `out_crs` parameter
The aggregate accepts an optional trailing `out_crs` (string CRS) argument. When supplied it overrides the integer `out_srid` for the output CRS. The JVM level takes `EPSG:<n>` (or a bare code) here and refuses other CRS text with a `Fix:`; the lightweight level reads any CRS string.
:::

:::tip Worked example
The [H3 Rasterize notebook](../notebooks/h3-rasterize) walks this through end to end on a San Francisco Bay Area DEM: elevation isobands → H3 polyfill → a shared canvas from [`rst_h3_gridspec`](#h3-grid) → per-band `rst_h3_rasterize_agg` → multi-band stack via [`rst_frombands_agg`](#rst_frombands_agg), visualized with the [`gbx.vizx`](./vizx) helpers. The same pattern maps directly to a telco multi-threshold signal-coverage stack.
:::

_Multi-row fixture: 3 H3 resolution-9 cell rows with burn values 1.0/2.0/3.0. All tabs use the same grouped-agg rasterize invocation._

| `cells` | `array<struct<cellid:bigint,value:double>>` |  | The group's cells as an array of structs `(cellid, value)`, for example `collect_list(struct(cellid, value))`. The rules of the aggregate hold: a repeated cell burns its largest value, a NULL cell id is skipped. |

Rasterize an array of H3 cells into one tile, the scalar twin of [`rst_h3_rasterize_agg`](#rst_h3_rasterize_agg). Collect the group's cells first, for example `gbx_rst_h3_rasterize(collect_list(struct(cellid, value)), ...)`. The answer is the same bytes as the aggregate gives. Use it for a group the aggregate's streaming shape cannot take, such as a Serverless finalize or a group from an earlier step. It is a session function, with no Unity Catalog form.

### rst_merge_agg

{/* BEGIN GENERATED gbx_rst_merge_agg: edit manifest/functions.json, then run gbx:manifest:gen */}
<Surfaces session python />

| Surface | Call | Returns |
|---|---|---|
| SQL (session) | `gbx_rst_merge_agg(tile)` | `BINARY` |
| Python | `rasterx.functions.rst_merge_agg(tile, virtualize_dir=None, virtualize_prefix=None, materialize=None)` | Column |

| Parameter | SQL type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `tile` | `TILE` |  | The tiles of the group. |
{/* END GENERATED gbx_rst_merge_agg */}

<Impl groupedAgg/>

:::note Lightweight tier (pyrx)
Powered by **rasterio** (`rasterio.merge`). Aggregate — `groupBy(...).agg(rx.rst_merge_agg("tile"))` merges the group's tiles into one mosaic tile (output spans the union extent). A tile in a different CRS is reprojected onto the pixel size and the pixel grid of a tile in the reference CRS. A mosaic built in memory and one streamed from disk (when it is too large for memory) then place the tiles on the same pixels. The reprojected tile can grow by up to a pixel on each side to stay on that grid. A tile reprojected from another CRS that declares no NoData (or has its own mask band) gets the footprint of the warp as its mask. The mosaic then has a mask band, and a pixel no tile reaches is invalid in it. A gap between tiles that are all in the reference CRS and declare no NoData is still filled with zeros. The heavyweight `gbx_rst_merge_agg` does not merge tiles in different CRSs: it returns one tile (the one that sorts first) and leaves the others out.
:::

:::warning `gbx_rst_merge_agg` returns BINARY
In SQL and Python the aggregate returns the raster as `BINARY` (the GeoTIFF bytes), not a tile. A grouped aggregate cannot return the tile struct there. The Scala function `rst_merge_agg` keeps returning the tile struct. Wrap the SQL or Python result with `gbx_rst_fromcontent` to get a tile back:

```sql
SELECT
  group_key AS cellid,
  gbx_rst_fromcontent(gbx_rst_merge_agg(tile), 'GTiff') AS tile
FROM tiles
GROUP BY group_key
```

The Python call `rx.rst_merge_agg(...)` returns the same `BINARY` column. Pass it to `rx.rst_fromcontent(col, "GTiff")` for a tile.
:::

Merge tiles per group.

_Multi-tile fixture: 3 per-band rows from `rgb_nir_small.tif`. All tabs use the same grouped-agg mosaic invocation._

### rst_merge_tvf

{/* BEGIN GENERATED gbx_rst_merge_tvf: edit manifest/functions.json, then run gbx:manifest:gen */}
<Surfaces session python />

| Surface | Call | Returns |
|---|---|---|
| SQL (session) | `gbx_rst_merge_tvf(rows)` | rows |
| Python | `rasterx.functions.rst_merge_tvf(df)` | DataFrame |

Rows: `key` `STRING`, `raster` `BINARY`.

| Parameter | SQL type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `rows` | `TABLE` |  | The tiles, as `TABLE(SELECT key, tile FROM ...) PARTITION BY (key)`: one mosaic per key. An error tile, an empty tile and a tile that does not open are left out. The `tile` column is a tile, not BINARY: wrap bytes with `gbx_rst_fromcontent`. A partition that holds more than one key raises. |
{/* END GENERATED gbx_rst_merge_tvf */}

Use it instead of [`rst_merge_agg`](#rst_merge_agg) for large groups: `SELECT * FROM gbx_rst_merge_tvf(TABLE(SELECT key, tile FROM tiles) PARTITION BY (key))`. It merges each key's tiles into the same pixels as the aggregate. It reads them a row at a time, not as one batch of the whole group. A key with no readable tile gives no row. A key whose mosaic is too large to build raises with a Fix that names the [VRT-mosaic](./vrt-mosaic) route (`mint_vrt`).

<Impl groupedAgg/>

If a cell id repeats in a group with different values, the **largest** value is burned, whatever order the rows arrive in. In the lightweight tier a null value (or `NaN`) never wins against a number. A cell whose values are all null burns `NaN`, also when the whole `value` column is null. Only an omitted `value` (or an untyped `NULL`) is the presence mask and burns `1.0`. A `DECIMAL` column reaches the aggregate without its type, so a column of `DECIMAL` nulls counts as omitted too: cast it to `DOUBLE` first.

:::note Lightweight tier (pyrx)
The lightweight implementation is backed by `pygx._quadbin` cell math and `rasterio` — it burns each quadbin cell's geometry/value into the output band via `rasterio.features.rasterize`, matching the heavyweight cell set and burn values exactly.
:::

:::warning NoData sentinel is -9999.0
Pixels not covered by any geometry in the group are set to **`-9999.0`** (band-registered NoData). Filter or mask downstream via `gbx_rst_getnodata` or `IS NULL` on the extracted band value.
:::

:::note The aggregate returns BINARY
In SQL and Python the aggregate returns the raster as `BINARY` (the GeoTIFF bytes), on every compute. Wrap it with `gbx_rst_fromcontent(<aggregate>, 'GTiff')` to get a tile back.
:::

Streaming aggregator that burns quadbin cell geometry/value pairs (one row per cell) into a single rasterized tile per group. Quadbin cells are defined in EPSG:4326 lon/lat; their geometry is reprojected to `out_srid`, so the output tile is in that CRS (`pixel_size`, the extent and `width`/`height` are given in it). The cell IDs carry their zoom level (0..26). The inverse of `rst_quadbin_rastertogrid*`: where those functions reduce raster pixels to per-cell statistics, this one synthesizes a raster from per-cell values.

:::note Lightweight-only `out_crs` parameter
The aggregate accepts an optional trailing `out_crs` (string CRS) argument. When supplied it overrides the integer `out_srid` for the output CRS. The JVM level takes `EPSG:<n>` (or a bare code) here and refuses other CRS text with a `Fix:`; the lightweight level reads any CRS string.
:::

_Multi-row fixture: 3 quadbin zoom-12 cell rows near central London with burn values 1.0/2.0/3.0. All tabs use the same grouped-agg rasterize invocation._

| Parameter | SQL type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `cells` | `array<struct<cellid:bigint,value:double>>` |  | The group's cells as an array of structs `(cellid, value)`, for example `collect_list(struct(cellid, value))`. The rules of the aggregate hold: a repeated cell burns its largest value, a NULL cell id is skipped. |
| `grid` | `struct<bound_x_min:bigint,bound_x_max:bigint,bound_y_min:bigint,bound_y_max:bigint,cell_splits:int,root_cell_size_x:int,root_cell_size_y:int,srid:int>` |  | The grid, from `gbx_custom_grid`. The same for every row of the group. |
| `out_srid` | `INT` |  | The EPSG code of the output CRS. |
| `pixel_size` | `DOUBLE` |  | The pixel size in CRS units; NULL takes it from the cells' resolution. |
| `xmin` | `DOUBLE` |  | The west edge of the canvas; NULL derives the canvas from the cells. |
| `ymin` | `DOUBLE` |  | The south edge of the canvas. |
| `xmax` | `DOUBLE` |  | The east edge of the canvas. |
| `ymax` | `DOUBLE` |  | The north edge of the canvas. |
| `width` | `INT` |  | The canvas width in pixels. |
| `height` | `INT` |  | The canvas height in pixels. |
| `mode` | `STRING` |  | How the canvas is derived when no extent is given: `'centroids'` or `'spatial_envelope'`. |
| `kring_pad` | `INT` |  | Rings of padding around the derived canvas. |
{/* END GENERATED gbx_rst_custom_rasterize */}

Rasterize an array of custom-grid cells into one tile, the scalar twin of [`rst_custom_rasterize_agg`](#rst_custom_rasterize_agg). Collect the group's cells first, for example `gbx_rst_custom_rasterize(collect_list(struct(cellid, value)), ...)`. The answer is the same bytes as the aggregate gives. Use it for a group the aggregate's streaming shape cannot take, such as a Serverless finalize or a group from an earlier step. It is a session function, with no Unity Catalog form.

### rst_rasterize_agg

{/* BEGIN GENERATED gbx_rst_rasterize_agg: edit manifest/functions.json, then run gbx:manifest:gen */}

| Python | `rasterx.functions.rst_rasterize_agg(geom, value, xmin, ymin, xmax, ymax, width_px, height_px, out_srid)` | Column |

| Parameter | SQL type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `geom` | `BINARY` |  | The feature geometry as WKB. A NULL one is skipped. |
| `value` | `DOUBLE` |  | The value burned where the geometry covers a pixel centre. A NULL value skips the row. |
| `xmin` | `DOUBLE` |  | The west edge of the output. |
| `ymin` | `DOUBLE` |  | The south edge. |
| `xmax` | `DOUBLE` |  | The east edge. |
| `ymax` | `DOUBLE` |  | The north edge. |
| `width_px` | `INT` |  | The output width in pixels. |
| `height_px` | `INT` |  | The output height in pixels. |
| `out_srid` | `INT` |  | The EPSG code of the output CRS. |
{/* END GENERATED gbx_rst_rasterize_agg */}

<Impl groupedAgg/>

:::note Lightweight tier (pyrx)
Powered by **rasterio** (`rasterio.features`). Aggregate — burns the group's `(geom, value)` rows into one tile over the given extent/size/SRID (last-wins on overlap).
:::

:::note The aggregate returns BINARY
In SQL and Python the aggregate returns the raster as `BINARY` (the GeoTIFF bytes), on every compute. Wrap it with `gbx_rst_fromcontent(<aggregate>, 'GTiff')` to get a tile back.
:::

Streaming aggregator that burns geometry/value pairs (one row per feature) into a single rasterized tile per group. Use it when features arrive as individual rows, not as a pre-built collection.

_Multi-row fixture: 3 polygon rows burned at values 1.0/2.0/3.0 over a `[0,0,4,4]` EPSG:4326 extent. All tabs use the same grouped-agg burn invocation._

## Constructor Functions

Create or load rasters from path, binary content, or bands (4 total).

### rst_dtmfromgeoms

{/* BEGIN GENERATED gbx_rst_dtmfromgeoms: edit manifest/functions.json, then run gbx:manifest:gen */}
<Surfaces session python />

| Surface | Call | Returns |
|---|---|---|
| SQL (session) | `gbx_rst_dtmfromgeoms(points_array, breaklines_array, merge_tolerance, snap_tolerance, xmin, ymin, xmax, ymax, width_px, height_px, [out_srid], [no_data], [out_crs])` | `TILE` |
| Python | `rasterx.functions.rst_dtmfromgeoms(points_array, breaklines_array, merge_tolerance, snap_tolerance, xmin, ymin, xmax, ymax, width_px, height_px, out_srid=None, no_data=None, out_crs=None)` | Column |

| Parameter | SQL type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `points_array` | `ARRAY<BINARY>` or `ARRAY<STRING>` |  | The points, each with a Z value: WKB, EWKB, WKT or EWKT, as an array. |
| `breaklines_array` | `ARRAY<BINARY>` or `ARRAY<STRING>` |  | Break lines, as an array; NULL or empty for none. The light level uses their vertices as extra points and does not enforce them as edges. |
| `merge_tolerance` | `DOUBLE` |  | Accepted for compatibility with the JVM level; not applied. |
| `snap_tolerance` | `DOUBLE` |  | Accepted for compatibility with the JVM level; not applied. |
| `xmin` | `DOUBLE` |  | The west edge of the output in the output CRS. |
| `ymin` | `DOUBLE` |  | The south edge of the output. |
| `xmax` | `DOUBLE` |  | The east edge of the output, above `xmin`. |
| `ymax` | `DOUBLE` |  | The north edge of the output, above `ymin`. |
| `width_px` | `INT` |  | The output width in pixels, above 0. |
| `height_px` | `INT` |  | The output height in pixels, above 0. |
| `out_srid` | `INT` | `NULL` | The EPSG or ESRI code of the output CRS. NULL leaves the output without a CRS, or with the geometry's own when `out_crs` is NULL too. |
| `no_data` | `DOUBLE` | `-9999.0` | The value of a pixel outside the points' hull. |
| `out_crs` | `STRING` | `NULL` | The output CRS as `EPSG:<n>`, `ESRI:<n>` or WKT. When set it wins over `out_srid`; setting both raises. |
{/* END GENERATED gbx_rst_dtmfromgeoms */}

:::note Lightweight tier (pyrx)
Powered by **rasterio** + **SciPy** (`scipy.spatial.Delaunay`). Barycentric interpolation over an **unconstrained** Delaunay TIN; cells outside the convex hull are NoData. `breaklines`, `merge_tolerance`, and `snap_tolerance` are accepted for signature parity but not enforced — the heavyweight tier builds a constrained TIN that honors them.
:::

Create a DTM raster tile via TIN/Delaunay interpolation from an array of Z-valued point WKB geometries, with an optional array of breakline WKB geometries to preserve sharp terrain transitions.

:::note Lightweight-only `out_crs` parameter
The lightweight **Python** binding accepts an optional trailing `out_crs` (string CRS) argument that the SQL and heavyweight/Scala tiers do not — the heavyweight `RST_DTMFromGeoms` builder is strictly ≤12-argument (`…, out_srid, [no_data]`) and rejects a 13th argument. In lightweight Python, `out_crs` (string) wins over the int `out_srid`. This is a lightweight superset, not a heavyweight regression.
:::

Output CRS via `out_srid` (int); input points assumed already in the output CRS. See [Coordinate Reference Systems](./coordinate-reference-systems#source-crs-vs-output-crs).

:::note Lightweight Python — virtual-tile force-output
The lightweight **Python** binding accepts three optional keyword arguments the SQL and heavyweight tiers do not — `virtualize_dir`, `virtualize_prefix`, and `materialize` — controlling whether the produced tile carries raster bytes or a bytes-free virtual reference. See [Virtual-tile force-output params](#virtual-tile-overrides).

_Constructor — stacks per-band tiles into a multi-band tile. Example splits the multiband fixture into per-band tiles via `rst_band`, then re-stacks them into a 3-band tile._

### rst_fromcontent

{/* BEGIN GENERATED gbx_rst_fromcontent: edit manifest/functions.json, then run gbx:manifest:gen */}
<Surfaces session python scala />

| Surface | Call | Returns |
|---|---|---|
| SQL (session) | `gbx_rst_fromcontent(content, driver)` | `TILE` |
| Python | `rasterx.functions.rst_fromcontent(content, driver)` | Column |

| Parameter | SQL type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `content` | `BINARY` |  | The raster file's bytes, for example the `content` column of the `binaryFile` reader. |
| `driver` | `STRING` |  | The GDAL driver short name the bytes are in, for example `'GTiff'`, in any case. It goes into the tile's `driver` metadata; a name that is no raster driver (no driver of that name, or a vector-only one such as `'GeoJSON'`) raises. |
{/* END GENERATED gbx_rst_fromcontent */}

:::note Lightweight tier (pyrx)
Powered by **rasterio**. The driver defaults to GTiff when unspecified and outputs are re-encoded as GeoTIFF; readable input formats are limited to the GDAL build bundled with rasterio.
:::

Create a raster from binary content.

**Returns:** Binary raster tile data

_Constructor — builds a tile from binary content. Example uses Spark's `binaryFile` reader to load bytes, then `rst_fromcontent` to decode them — this is the canonical tier-agnostic pattern (works on any compute including Serverless)._

:::caution Serverless memory — you own the bytes
`rst_fromcontent` materializes bytes that are **already in a column**. Those bytes are in executor memory before this call; GeoBrix does not guard their size. On Serverless / Spark Connect the connect-aware cap is **64 MiB** per tile — keep individual `content` values at or under that limit. For larger sources, use the virtual-tile raster readers (the default) and let the runtime page bytes lazily. See [Serverless & Memory](../serverless-and-memory#rst_fromcontent-you-own-the-bytes).
:::

### rst_fromfile

{/* BEGIN GENERATED gbx_rst_fromfile: edit manifest/functions.json, then run gbx:manifest:gen */}
<Surfaces session python />

| Surface | Call | Returns |
|---|---|---|
| SQL (session) | `gbx_rst_fromfile(path, [driver])` | `TILE` |
| Python | `rasterx.functions.rst_fromfile(path, driver=None, materialize=None)` | Column |

| Parameter | SQL type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `path` | `STRING` |  | The path of the raster file: local, `/Volumes/...` or `/Workspace/...`. A remote URL is refused unless the driver's remote-read switch is on. |
| `driver` | `STRING` | `'GTiff'` | The GDAL driver short name hint, in any case. It goes into the tile's `driver` metadata; a name that is no raster driver (no driver of that name, or a vector-only one such as `'GeoJSON'`) raises. |
{/* END GENERATED gbx_rst_fromfile */}

Read a raster file by path into a tile.

In Python, `rst_fromfile` returns a _virtual_ tile by default. It holds no bytes, points at `path` over the whole-file window and reads `width`, `height` and the CRS from the header. No pixel is read until a later function needs it, so it is the way to load large rasters lazily. Pass `materialize=True` to read the pixels now. There is no `virtualize_dir` or `virtualize_prefix` argument, because the source file is the durable store.

The SQL function `gbx_rst_fromfile` always reads the file's pixels and returns a tile that holds them, so every function accepts it. It is a Python function: it needs the lightweight tier (the env wheel, or the plain wheel off Databricks) and has no Scala form. The JVM cannot read a UC Volume `/Volumes/...` path, because the credential lives in the Spark Python worker. A path that cannot be opened gives null.

:::note Remote paths
A remote URL (`http(s)://`, `/vsicurl/`, ...) is data here. `rst_fromfile` refuses it at its first evaluation, with the location and a `Fix:`. Set `GBX_ALLOW_REMOTE_PATHS=true` in the driver's environment before `register()` and before the column is built to allow it (see [Security § 7](../security#7-raster-data-other-principals-can-write)).
:::

:::caution `materialize=True` errors over the Serverless cap
Reading a file larger than the connect-aware stream cap raises a `ValueError`. The cap is **64 MiB on Serverless / Spark Connect** and 256 MiB on classic. This applies to `materialize=True` and to the SQL function `gbx_rst_fromfile`. The error is intentional, because reading an oversized tile would run the executor out of memory. The Python default (`materialize` unset, a virtual tile) is Serverless-safe: pixels are read lazily and size-gated at each later function. See [Serverless & Memory](../serverless-and-memory#rst_fromfile-materialize-true).
:::

<FunctionExamples
  name="rst_fromfile"
  sql={rasterxSqlCode}                  sqlSource="docs/tests/python/api/rasterx_functions_sql.py"
  python={rasterxTileopsLightCode}  pythonSource="docs/tests/python/api/rasterx_tileops_python_light.py"
  testFile="docs/tests/python/api/test_rasterx_tileops_python_light.py"
/>

:::tip Loading rasters at scale? Use the [Raster Reader](../readers/raster)
`rst_fromfile` pulls columnar raster paths into a tile column inline. To ingest rasters as a normal Spark job — partitioned parallel reads, optional tiling (`sizeInMB`), FUSE-safe Volume staging — use the **[Raster Reader](../readers/raster)** (`raster_gbx` / `gtiff_gbx`, or heavyweight `gdal` / `gtiff_gdal`): `spark.read.format("raster_gbx").load(path)`. It, too, emits virtual tiles by default.
:::

:::note Have the bytes already, or need a materialized tile? Use `rst_fromcontent`
If you already hold raster bytes in a column (e.g. from Spark's built-in `binaryFile` reader), build the tile with [`gbx_rst_fromcontent(content, driver)`](#rst_fromcontent). Note this is **not** a lazy equivalent of `rst_fromfile` in the lightweight tier: `rst_fromcontent` takes bytes you have already read, so it always produces a **materialized** tile. (In the heavyweight tier all tiles are materialized, so there the two are interchangeable.) It also works without the lightweight tier and on any compute, since the `binaryFile` reader runs in Spark, which holds the Volume credential:

```python
df = (
    spark.read.format("binaryFile")
    .load("/Volumes/main/geobrix_samples/geobrix-examples/nyc/*.tif")
    .selectExpr("path", "gbx_rst_fromcontent(content, 'GTiff') AS tile")
)
```
:::

Powered by **rasterio** + **SciPy** (`cKDTree` IDW). Inverse-distance interpolation (`power`, `max_pts`) to a single-band Float64 grid; NoData −9999. Matches the heavyweight `invdist` defaults (`power=2.0`, `max_pts=12`).

IDW-interpolate an array of Z-valued point geometries to a Float64 GeoTIFF tile covering an explicit bounding box and pixel grid. Supply the points and their scalar values as arrays in a single row; use `rst_gridfrompoints_agg` when points arrive one per row.

The lightweight **Python** binding accepts an optional trailing `out_crs` (string CRS) argument that the SQL and heavyweight/Scala tiers do not — the heavyweight `RST_GridFromPoints` builder is strictly ≤11-argument (`…, out_srid, [power, [max_pts]]`) and rejects a 12th argument. In lightweight Python, `out_crs` (string) wins over the int `out_srid`. This is a lightweight superset, not a heavyweight regression.

## LiDAR / Point-Cloud DSM {#lidar-dsm}

Four functions for building raster products from LiDAR point clouds. Available on **both tiers** (`pyrx` lightweight and `rasterx` heavyweight). Load point clouds with the [`lidar_gbx` reader](../readers/lidar). For an end-to-end walkthrough — `.laz` → DSM/DTM binning → Canopy Height Model — see the [Wireless Coverage example](../notebooks/wireless-coverage).

### rst_binpoints

{/* BEGIN GENERATED gbx_rst_binpoints: edit manifest/functions.json, then run gbx:manifest:gen */}
<Surfaces session python scala />

| Surface | Call | Returns |
|---|---|---|
| SQL (session) | `gbx_rst_binpoints(x_array, y_array, z_array, xmin, ymin, xmax, ymax, width_px, height_px, srid, [statistic])` | `TILE` |
| Python | `rasterx.functions.rst_binpoints(x_array, y_array, z_array, xmin, ymin, xmax, ymax, width_px, height_px, srid, statistic=None)` | Column |

| Parameter | SQL type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `x_array` | `ARRAY<DOUBLE>` |  | The x coordinate of each point. |
| `y_array` | `ARRAY<DOUBLE>` |  | The y coordinate of each point, in the same order. |
| `z_array` | `ARRAY<DOUBLE>` |  | The value of each point, in the same order. |
| `xmin` | `DOUBLE` |  | The west edge of the output in the output CRS. |
| `ymin` | `DOUBLE` |  | The south edge of the output. |
| `xmax` | `DOUBLE` |  | The east edge of the output, above `xmin`. |
| `ymax` | `DOUBLE` |  | The north edge of the output, above `ymin`. |
| `width_px` | `INT` |  | The output width in pixels, above 0. |
| `height_px` | `INT` |  | The output height in pixels, above 0. |
| `srid` | `INT` |  | The EPSG or ESRI code of the output CRS. |
| `statistic` | `STRING` | `'max'` | How the points in a pixel combine: `max`, `min`, `mean`, `median`, `count` or `percentile:<p>` (for example `percentile:90`), in any case. Another word raises. |
{/* END GENERATED gbx_rst_binpoints */}

Bin a **per-row `ARRAY<DOUBLE>`** of (x, y, z) coordinate triples into a single-band
Float32 raster tile. Each pixel carries the reduction of all points whose centroid falls
within the pixel cell. Empty cells carry NoData (−9999). The bounding box uses a
half-open interval on the upper x/y edges to prevent double-counting when DSM tiles
are assembled from adjacent strips.

**What it computes.** This is the point-cloud→raster (DSM-building) step: every input
point is dropped into the grid cell its (x, y) falls in, and the pixel value is the chosen
reduction of the z-values that landed there. With the default `"max"` that is the *tallest*
return per cell — a surface model (DSM), exactly the input [`rst_chm`](#rst_chm) differences
against a DEM; `"min"`/`"mean"`/`"median"`/`"count"`/`"percentile:<p>"` reduce the same
points into other surfaces.

  testFile="docs/tests/python/api/test_rasterx_tileops_python_light.py"
/>

### rst_binpoints_agg

| Surface | Call | Returns |

| Python | `rasterx.functions.rst_binpoints_agg(x, y, z, xmin, ymin, xmax, ymax, width_px, height_px, srid, statistic=None)` | Column |

| Parameter | SQL type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `x` | `DOUBLE` |  | The point's x. |
| `y` | `DOUBLE` |  | The point's y. |
| `z` | `DOUBLE` |  | The point's value. |
| `xmin` | `DOUBLE` |  | The west edge of the output. |
| `ymin` | `DOUBLE` |  | The south edge. |
| `xmax` | `DOUBLE` |  | The east edge. |
| `ymax` | `DOUBLE` |  | The north edge. |
| `width_px` | `INT` |  | The output width in pixels. |
| `height_px` | `INT` |  | The output height in pixels. |
| `srid` | `INT` |  | The EPSG code of the output CRS. |
| `statistic` | `STRING` | `'max'` | `'max'`, `'min'`, `'mean'`, `'median'`, `'count'` or `'percentile:<p>'`. |
{/* END GENERATED gbx_rst_binpoints_agg */}

<Impl groupedAgg/>

Aggregator variant of [`rst_binpoints`](#rst_binpoints): stream **one scalar (x, y, z) point
per row** into a DSM tile via `groupBy(...).agg(rx.rst_binpoints_agg(...))`. The
`xmin`/`ymin`/`xmax`/`ymax`/`width_px`/`height_px`/`srid`/`statistic` arguments are
per-group constants. The per-cell binning is identical to [`rst_binpoints`](#rst_binpoints) —
see **What it computes** there for the reduction semantics and statistic modes — it just
streams one point per row instead of a per-row array.

The lightweight SQL aggregate returns **`BINARY`** (raster bytes). Wrap with
`gbx_rst_fromcontent(<agg>, 'GTiff')` to rebuild a tile struct. The lightweight Python
wrapper `rx.rst_binpoints_agg(...)` returns the full tile struct.

<FunctionExamples
  name="rst_binpoints_agg"
  sql={rasterxSqlCode}                     sqlSource="docs/tests/python/api/rasterx_functions_sql.py"
  python={rasterxAggregatorsLightCode} pythonSource="docs/tests/python/api/rasterx_aggregators_python_light.py"
  scala={scalaApiExamplesCode}             scalaSource="docs/tests/scala/api/ScalaApiExamples.scala"
  testFile="docs/tests/python/api/test_rasterx_aggregators_python_light.py"
/>

:::note Memory & scale — binning full-density LiDAR

Both `rst_binpoints_agg` (grouped — realizes a whole group's points in the worker) and
`rst_binpoints` (scalar — realizes a row's point arrays) hold memory **proportional to the
points per group**. That is fine at moderate density, but a full-resolution tile (millions of
returns — e.g. a 1 km tile of 1 m LiDAR) can exhaust worker RAM.

For large or full-density point clouds, use the bounded light-tier helper
**`rx.bin_points_tiled(df, x, y, z, by=[…], xmin=…, ymin=…, xmax=…, ymax=…, width, height, srid, stat)`**.
It runs a **two-stage native aggregation** — per-point pixel index → native `groupBy(tile, cell)`
reduction → placement — so peak memory is the **raster grid (W×H)**, independent of point count.
It returns the same tile struct (drops straight into `rst_chm`), and its output is **identical** to
`groupBy(by).agg(rst_binpoints_agg(…))` for `max`/`min`/`mean`/`count`. `median`/`percentile` (which
need every value in a cell) delegate to the buffered aggregator — decimate or use smaller tiles for
those. Prefer `bin_points_tiled` over a `collect_list(all points) → rst_binpoints` pattern, which
buffers the whole cloud. See [Performance](./performance) and the [LiDAR reader](../readers/lidar).

:::

---

### rst_isoband

{/* BEGIN GENERATED gbx_rst_isoband: edit manifest/functions.json, then run gbx:manifest:gen */}
<Surfaces session python scala />

| Surface | Call | Returns |
|---|---|---|
| SQL (session) | `gbx_rst_isoband(tile, breaks)` | `array<struct<geom_wkb:binary,band:int,lower:double,upper:double>>` |
| Python | `rasterx.functions.rst_isoband(tile, breaks)` | Column |

| Parameter | SQL type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `tile` | `TILE` |  | The tile to classify. |
| `breaks` | `ARRAY<DOUBLE>` |  | The N+1 boundaries of N bands, strictly ascending. Band i holds the pixels from `breaks[i]` up to, not including, `breaks[i+1]`. |
{/* END GENERATED gbx_rst_isoband */}

Reclassify a raster band into value bins defined by a `breaks` array and return one
polygon per **contiguous patch** of pixels in the same bin. Useful for producing
height-band polygons from a CHM or DSM, or elevation isobands from a DEM.

Returns `ARRAY<STRUCT<geom_wkb BINARY, band INT, lower DOUBLE, upper DOUBLE>>` — one
entry per contiguous region. `geom_wkb` is a WKB Polygon in the raster's own CRS.
NoData pixels are excluded. Pixels below `breaks[0]` or `>= breaks[-1]` are dropped.

Each bin is **half-open — `[breaks[i], breaks[i+1])`** — closed on the lower bound and
open on the upper, so a value equal to a break belongs to the band that break *opens*.
For example, with `breaks = array(0, 50, 100, 150, 200)`, a value of `55.0` — and `50.0`
itself — falls in the `[50.0, 100.0)` band (`band` index 1), while `100.0` moves up to
the `[100.0, 150.0)` band and `200.0` (equal to `breaks[-1]`) is dropped.

Implemented with `numpy.digitize` + `rasterio.features.shapes` — no GDAL required.

<FunctionExamples
  name="rst_isoband"
  sql={rasterxSqlCode}                  sqlSource="docs/tests/python/api/rasterx_functions_sql.py"
  python={rasterxTileopsLightCode}  pythonSource="docs/tests/python/api/rasterx_tileops_python_light.py"

### rst_chm

{/* BEGIN GENERATED gbx_rst_chm: edit manifest/functions.json, then run gbx:manifest:gen */}
<Surfaces session python />

| Surface | Call | Returns |
|---|---|---|
| SQL (session) | `gbx_rst_chm(dsm_tile, dem_tile)` | `TILE` |
| Python | `rasterx.functions.rst_chm(dsm_tile, dem_tile)` | Column |

| Parameter | SQL type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `dsm_tile` | `TILE` |  | The Digital Surface Model tile. |
| `dem_tile` | `TILE` |  | The Digital Elevation Model tile; it defines the output grid. |
{/* END GENERATED gbx_rst_chm */}

Compute a **Canopy Height Model (CHM)** from a Digital Surface Model (DSM) tile and a
Digital Elevation Model (DEM) tile: `CHM = clamp(align(DSM → DEM grid) − DEM, min=0)`.

**What it computes.** The DSM holds the height of the *top* surface in each cell (canopy,
rooftops) and the DEM holds bare-earth ground elevation, so subtracting one from the other
leaves the height of whatever stands above the ground — canopy or object height — in the
DEM's units. It is a per-cell **subtraction**, not an aggregation: taking the max z per cell
is how you build the DSM upstream (e.g. `rst_binpoints` over LiDAR returns), and `rst_chm`
then differences that surface against the DEM.

The DSM is warped onto the DEM's grid (CRS, extent, width, height) via
nearest-neighbour resampling before subtraction. Negative differences (where the DSM
dips below the DEM due to noise) are clamped to 0. NoData in either input propagates
to the output. Output is a Float32 GeoTIFF tile with NoData −9999.

<FunctionExamples
  name="rst_chm"
  sql={rasterxSqlCode}                  sqlSource="docs/tests/python/api/rasterx_functions_sql.py"
  python={rasterxTileopsLightCode}  pythonSource="docs/tests/python/api/rasterx_tileops_python_light.py"
  testFile="docs/tests/python/api/test_rasterx_tileops_python_light.py"
/>

## Generator Functions

Produce multiple tiles or bands (7 total).

The generators are table functions: call them in the `FROM` clause with `LATERAL`, at every implementation level. The Python entry takes the input DataFrame first and returns it joined with the generated rows, for example `rasterx.functions.rst_retile(df, "tile", 512, 512)`.

### rst_bng_tessellate

{/* BEGIN GENERATED gbx_rst_bng_tessellate: edit manifest/functions.json, then run gbx:manifest:gen */}
<Surfaces session python scala />

| Surface | Call | Returns |
|---|---|---|
| SQL (session) | `gbx_rst_bng_tessellate(tile, resolution, [assignment], [coverage])` | rows |
| Python | `rasterx.functions.rst_bng_tessellate(df, tile, resolution, assignment=None, coverage=None)` | DataFrame |

Rows: `cellid` `BIGINT`, `raster` `BINARY`, `path` `STRING`, `path_mode` `STRING`, `window` `struct<col_off:int,row_off:int,width:int,height:int>`, `clip_polygon` `BINARY`, `clip_crs` `STRING`, `crs` `STRING`, `metadata` `map<string,string>`.

| Parameter | SQL type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `tile` | `TILE` |  | The tile to split. |
| `resolution` | `INT` or `STRING` |  | BNG resolution: an index (1 to 6, negative for a quadrant) or a size such as `'1km'` or `'100m'`. |
| `assignment` | `STRING` | `'centroid'` | How a pixel joins a cell: `'centroid'` (the cell holds the pixel's centre) or `'covering'` (every cell the pixel overlaps). Any other word raises. |
| `coverage` | `STRING` | `'complete'` | `'complete'` also gives the cells that overlap the raster but hold no valid pixel, as all-NoData tiles; `'sparse'` leaves them out. Any other word raises. |
{/* END GENERATED gbx_rst_bng_tessellate */}

<Impl udtf/>

:::note Lightweight tier (pyrx)
The lightweight implementation is backed by `pygx._bng` cell math and `rasterio`. It auto-warps the input to **EPSG:27700** via `rasterio.warp`, enumerates the overlapping BNG cells, and clips one tile per cell — rendering **STRING** BNG cell IDs at the row boundary and matching the heavyweight cell set and clip windows.
:::

Tessellate a raster to British National Grid (BNG) cells — one row per overlapping cell, each clipped to that cell's extent. The raster is automatically reprojected to **EPSG:27700** (British National Grid) before tessellation, so any projected or geographic input is handled transparently. Resolution accepts integer indices **±1..±6** (1 = 100 km, 2 = 10 km, 3 = 1 km, 4 = 100 m, 5 = 10 m, 6 = 1 m; negative indices address quadrant sub-cells) or string keys from `BNG.resolutionMap` (e.g. `"1km"`, `"100m"`). Cell IDs are **STRING** (e.g. `"TQ28"`, `"SU3412"`).

Natural fit for CV image-tiling workflows that require Ordnance Survey–scale cells aligned to the national grid — for example tiling aerial or satellite imagery into 1 km BNG cells for object-detection model inference. See issue [#49](https://github.com/databrickslabs/geobrix/issues/49) for the background.

Assignment is `"centroid"` (default) or `"covering"`; coverage is `"complete"` (default) or `"sparse"`.

**SQL:**

### rst_h3_tessellate

{/* BEGIN GENERATED gbx_rst_h3_tessellate: edit manifest/functions.json, then run gbx:manifest:gen */}
<Surfaces session python scala />

| Surface | Call | Returns |
|---|---|---|
| SQL (session) | `gbx_rst_h3_tessellate(tile, resolution, [assignment], [coverage])` | rows |
| Python | `rasterx.functions.rst_h3_tessellate(df, tile, resolution, assignment=None, coverage=None)` | DataFrame |

Rows: `cellid` `BIGINT`, `raster` `BINARY`, `path` `STRING`, `path_mode` `STRING`, `window` `struct<col_off:int,row_off:int,width:int,height:int>`, `clip_polygon` `BINARY`, `clip_crs` `STRING`, `crs` `STRING`, `metadata` `map<string,string>`.

| Parameter | SQL type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `tile` | `TILE` |  | The tile to split. |
| `resolution` | `INT` |  | The H3 resolution, a whole number from 0 to 15. |
| `assignment` | `STRING` | `'centroid'` | How a pixel joins a cell: `'centroid'` (the cell holds the pixel's centre) or `'covering'` (every cell the pixel overlaps). Any other word raises. |
| `coverage` | `STRING` | `'complete'` | `'complete'` also gives the cells that overlap the raster but hold no valid pixel, as all-NoData tiles; `'sparse'` leaves them out. Any other word raises. |
{/* END GENERATED gbx_rst_h3_tessellate */}

<Impl udtf/>

:::note Lightweight tier (pyrx)
Powered by **rasterio** + **h3**. Clips the raster to each overlapping H3 cell at `resolution`, returning one row per cell via streaming UDTF (matches the heavyweight generator behavior).
:::

Tessellate raster to H3 cells. assignment is `"centroid"` (default — pixel-centroid single-assignment) or `"covering"` (every overlapping cell, clipped); coverage is `"complete"` (default) or `"sparse"`. See [Grid raster tessellation](./grid-raster-tessellation) for the full guide.

Covering mode uses a **positive-area overlap** rule (shared by all three grids — `rst_h3_tessellate`, `rst_quadbin_tessellate`, `rst_bng_tessellate`): a cell is emitted **iff its geometry has greater-than-zero area overlap** with the raster. A cell that merely touches the raster along a boundary edge or corner (zero pixel overlap — common on grid-aligned tiles where the raster edges land on cell boundaries) is **excluded**. A within-extent cell whose pixels are all NoData **is** emitted — NoData renders in place, it does not punch a gap into the mosaic. This rule is identical on the lightweight and heavyweight tiers, so covering-mode cell sets match exactly across tiers.

Tessellate a raster to CARTO quadbin v0 cells — one row per overlapping cell, each clipped to that cell's extent. A raster in another CRS is accepted: its footprint is reprojected to EPSG:4326 to find the overlapping cells (see [Grid raster tessellation](./grid-raster-tessellation) for the antimeridian and pole limits). Resolution is the quadbin zoom level (0..26). Each output row carries the quadbin cell ID (`BIGINT`) and the clipped tile struct.

{/* END GENERATED gbx_rst_tooverlappingtiles */}

<Impl udtf/>

:::note Lightweight tier (pyrx)
Powered by **rasterio**. Streams one tile row per overlapping region via streaming UDTF. Every window is emitted, including windows whose pixels are all NoData, in both tiers. To drop them, filter with `WHERE NOT gbx_rst_isempty(tile)` or write with `pruneEmpty`.
:::

Create overlapping tiles.
