## Grid Functions (H3) {#h3-grid}

See [Grid raster tessellation](./grid-raster-tessellation) for the coverage / assignment / fill model shared by all grids.

Aggregate raster values to H3 grid cells, and utility functions for H3-based canvas setup (9 total).

The `rst_<grid>_rastertogrid<stat>` reducers of all four grids (H3, quadbin, BNG, custom grid) are **table functions** at every implementation level. They return one row per band and cell, with `band` (from 1), `cellID` and `measure`. Call them in the `FROM` clause with `LATERAL`, or through the Python API, which takes the input DataFrame first and returns it joined with the rows:

```python
from databricks.labs.gbx.rasterx import functions as rx

rx.rst_h3_rastertogridavg(df, "tile", 5)  # one row per band and H3 cell, beside the input columns
```

A NULL tile, an error tile and a tile that cannot be read give no rows. A resolution the grid does not define raises and names the value. A `coverage` or `assignment` word that names none does the same. Cell ids are `BIGINT` (H3, quadbin, custom grid) and never pass through a float; BNG ids are `STRING`.

### h3_cell_bbox

{/* BEGIN GENERATED gbx_h3_cell_bbox: edit manifest/functions.json, then run gbx:manifest:gen */}
<Surfaces session python scala />

| Surface | Call | Returns |
|---|---|---|
| SQL (session) | `gbx_h3_cell_bbox(cellid, [srid], [mode], [kring_pad], [out_crs])` | `struct<xmin:double,ymin:double,xmax:double,ymax:double>` |
| Python | `gridx.h3.functions.h3_cell_bbox(cellid, srid=None, mode=None, kring_pad=None, out_crs=None)` | Column |

| Parameter | SQL type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `cellid` | `BIGINT` |  | The H3 cell id. |
| `srid` | `INT` | `4326` | The EPSG code of the output coordinates. |
| `mode` | `STRING` | `'centroids'` | `'centroids'` wraps the cell centres, `'spatial_envelope'` the whole hexagons. |
| `kring_pad` | `INT` | `0` | How many rings of neighbours to include before taking the box; 0 is the cell alone. |
| `out_crs` | `STRING` | `NULL` | The output coordinate system as `EPSG:<n>`, `ESRI:<n>` or WKT; when set it wins over `srid`. |
{/* END GENERATED gbx_h3_cell_bbox */}

:::note Lightweight tier (pyrx)
Powered by **h3**. Returns a `STRUCT<xmin DOUBLE, ymin DOUBLE, xmax DOUBLE, ymax DOUBLE>` bounding box for the given H3 cell in the requested `srid`. In `'centroids'` mode the box tightly wraps the centroid point; in `'spatial_envelope'` mode it wraps the full hexagon outline. When `kring_pad > 0` the k-ring of that radius is computed first and the bounding box covers all cells in the ring.
:::

Scalar function — returns the bounding box `STRUCT<xmin, ymin, xmax, ymax>` for one H3 cell in the given CRS. Use this to drive the `xmin/ymin/xmax/ymax` and grid-size parameters of [`rst_h3_rasterize_agg`](#rst_h3_rasterize_agg) when you need a consistent per-cell canvas, or to clip and inspect cell extents in downstream queries.

**Returns:** `STRUCT<xmin DOUBLE, ymin DOUBLE, xmax DOUBLE, ymax DOUBLE>`

**SQL:**

<FunctionExamples
  name="h3_cell_bbox"
  sql={rasterxSqlCode}                  sqlSource="docs/tests/python/api/rasterx_functions_sql.py"
  python={rasterxGridaggLightCode} pythonSource="docs/tests/python/api/rasterx_gridagg_python_light.py"
  scala={scalaApiExamplesCode}          scalaSource="docs/tests/scala/api/ScalaApiExamples.scala"
  testFile="docs/tests/python/api/test_rasterx_gridagg_python_light.py"
/>

### rst_h3_gridspec (Python / DataFrame helper)

:::note Lightweight (pyrx) Python helper only
`rst_h3_gridspec` is **not registered as a SQL function** and is **not available in the heavyweight tier**. It is a pure-Python / PySpark DataFrame helper in the `pyrx` package.

For the heavyweight tier, compose the equivalent shared canvas using the registered scalar `gbx_h3_cell_bbox(cellid, srid, mode, kring_pad)` with native Spark `min`/`max` aggregates and the same floor/ceil snap arithmetic:
```sql
SELECT min(cell_bbox.xmin), min(cell_bbox.ymin),
       max(cell_bbox.xmax), max(cell_bbox.ymax)
FROM   (SELECT gbx_h3_cell_bbox(cellid, 4326, 'centroids', 1) AS cell_bbox FROM cells)
```
:::

`rst_h3_gridspec` computes the shared, snapped canvas (extent + pixel size) that a group of H3 cells should use so that per-threshold rasters align on a common grid and can be stacked via `rst_frombands_agg` or mosaicked via `rst_merge_agg`.

**Signature:**
```python
rx.rst_h3_gridspec(df, cell_col="cellid", *group_cols,
                   srid=4326, pixel_size=None,
                   mode="centroids", kring_pad=1)
```

**Typical multi-threshold workflow:**

1. Call `rx.rst_h3_gridspec(df, cell_col="cellid", srid=4326, mode='centroids', kring_pad=1)` once on the distinct cell set. It returns the grouped DataFrame with a `grid` struct column — one row per group containing the shared canvas.
2. For each threshold band, run `rst_h3_rasterize_agg` with those fixed bounds — all output tiles share the same origin and pixel grid.
3. Stack aligned bands with `rst_frombands_agg` (ordered by `band_index`), or mosaic per-cell tiles with `rst_merge_agg`.

**Parameters:**
- `df` — input Spark DataFrame containing H3 cell IDs
- `cell_col` — column name holding H3 cell IDs (integer or string); default `"cellid"`
- `*group_cols` — additional grouping columns (e.g. a transmitter ID, year, month); one grid spec row is produced per group
- `srid` — EPSG code for the output CRS; `4326` for WGS 84 (default)
- `pixel_size` — ground resolution in CRS units; `None` = auto-derived from the H3 resolution via an edge-length heuristic (default)
- `mode` — `'centroids'` (default) or `'spatial_envelope'`
- `kring_pad` — k-ring expansion applied per cell before computing its bounding box (default `1`)

**Returns:** the grouped DataFrame with a `grid` column of type:
```
STRUCT<xmin DOUBLE, ymin DOUBLE, xmax DOUBLE, ymax DOUBLE,
       pixel_size DOUBLE, width INT, height INT, srid INT>
```

### rst_h3_rastertogridavg

{/* BEGIN GENERATED gbx_rst_h3_rastertogridavg: edit manifest/functions.json, then run gbx:manifest:gen */}
<Surfaces session python />

| Surface | Call | Returns |
|---|---|---|
| SQL (session) | `gbx_rst_h3_rastertogridavg(tile, resolution, [coverage], [assignment])` | rows |
| Python | `rasterx.functions.rst_h3_rastertogridavg(df, tile, resolution, coverage=None, assignment=None)` | DataFrame |

Rows: `band` `INT`, `cellID` `BIGINT`, `measure` `DOUBLE`.

| Parameter | SQL type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `tile` | `TILE` |  | The tile to aggregate. |
| `resolution` | `INT` |  | The H3 resolution, a whole number from 0 to 15. |
| `coverage` | `STRING` | `'complete'` | `'complete'` also gives the cells that overlap the raster but hold no valid pixel, with a NULL measure (0 for a count); `'sparse'` gives only the cells that hold a valid pixel. Any other word raises. |
| `assignment` | `STRING` | `'centroid'` | How a pixel joins a cell: `'centroid'` (the cell holds the pixel's centre) or `'covering'` (every cell the pixel overlaps, in proportion to the overlapping area). Any other word raises. |
{/* END GENERATED gbx_rst_h3_rastertogridavg */}

## Grid Functions (quadbin)

See [Grid raster tessellation](./grid-raster-tessellation) for the coverage / assignment / fill model shared by all grids.

Aggregate raster values to CARTO quadbin v0 grid cells (8 reducers). Each reducer is a table function with one row per band and cell (`band`, `cellID` BIGINT, `measure` DOUBLE). Resolution is the quadbin zoom (0..20). See also [`rst_quadbin_tessellate`](#rst_quadbin_tessellate) (Generator Functions) and [`rst_quadbin_rasterize_agg`](#rst_quadbin_rasterize_agg) (Aggregator Functions) for the remaining quadbin raster-grid surface.

| SQL (session) | `gbx_rst_quadbin_rastertogridmax(tile, resolution, [coverage], [assignment])` | rows |
| Python | `rasterx.functions.rst_quadbin_rastertogridmax(df, tile, resolution, coverage=None, assignment=None)` | DataFrame |

## Grid Functions (BNG) {#bng-grid}

See [Grid raster tessellation](./grid-raster-tessellation) for the coverage / assignment / fill model shared by all grids.

Aggregate raster values to British National Grid (BNG) cells (8 total). BNG functions automatically reproject the input raster to **EPSG:27700** before sampling — no `rst_transform` needed upstream. Resolution accepts integer indices **±1..±6** (1 = 100 km, 2 = 10 km, 3 = 1 km, 4 = 100 m, 5 = 10 m, 6 = 1 m; negative indices address quadrant sub-cells) or string keys (e.g. `"1km"`, `"100m"`). Cell IDs are **STRING**.

:::warning NoData and NULL
With the default `complete` coverage, a cell that overlaps the raster but holds only NoData pixels is returned with a **`NULL`** measure (`0` for a count; never `NaN`). The H3 and quadbin reducers behave the same. `NULL` is safe to filter (`WHERE measure IS NULL`) and is excluded by SQL aggregates like `MAX`/`MIN`/`AVG`.
:::

## Grid Functions (Custom Grid) {#custom-grid}

See [Grid raster tessellation](./grid-raster-tessellation) for the coverage / assignment / fill model shared by all grids.

Aggregate raster values to user-defined custom-grid cells (8 total). Custom grids are defined via `gbx_custom_grid(origin_x, origin_y, min_x, min_y, max_x, max_y, root_cell_size_x, root_cell_size_y, crs_srid)` — a regular planar grid with an explicit origin, bounds, and cell size. Cell IDs are **BIGINT** encoded by the custom-grid layout, and the resolution parameter selects the subdivision level. The raster is expected to be in the grid's native CRS — no automatic reprojection is performed; use `rst_transform` upstream if needed.

:::warning NoData and NULL
With the default `complete` coverage, a cell that overlaps the raster but holds only NoData pixels is returned with a **`NULL`** measure (`0` for a count; never `NaN`). The H3, quadbin, and BNG reducers behave the same. `NULL` is safe to filter (`WHERE measure IS NULL`) and is excluded by SQL aggregates like `MAX`/`MIN`/`AVG`.
:::

## Operations

Transform and analyze rasters (20 total).

### rst_align_to

{/* BEGIN GENERATED gbx_rst_align_to: edit manifest/functions.json, then run gbx:manifest:gen */}
<Surfaces session python scala />

| Surface | Call | Returns |
|---|---|---|
| SQL (session) | `gbx_rst_align_to(tile, reference_tile)` | `TILE` |
| Python | `rasterx.functions.rst_align_to(tile, reference_tile, virtualize_dir=None, virtualize_prefix=None, materialize=None)` | Column |

| Parameter | SQL type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `tile` | `TILE` |  | The tile to warp. |
| `reference_tile` | `TILE` |  | The tile whose grid (CRS, extent, width, height) the result takes. |
{/* END GENERATED gbx_rst_align_to */}

:::note Lightweight tier (pyrx)
Powered by **rasterio** (`rasterio.warp`). Warps `tile` onto the reference tile's grid — matching its CRS, extent, and pixel dimensions — using nearest-neighbour resampling. Satisfies the alignment precondition required by the `rst_combine*` family.
:::

Warp a tile to match the grid of a reference tile exactly — same CRS, extent, width, height, and geotransform — using nearest-neighbour resampling (`-r near`). This is the explicit fix for the alignment precondition of the cross-raster combine family (`rst_combine*`): align each tile to a common reference, then combine.

### rst_asformat

{/* BEGIN GENERATED gbx_rst_asformat: edit manifest/functions.json, then run gbx:manifest:gen */}
<Surfaces session python scala />

| Surface | Call | Returns |
|---|---|---|
| SQL (session) | `gbx_rst_asformat(tile, new_format)` | `TILE` |
| Python | `rasterx.functions.rst_asformat(tile, new_format, virtualize_dir=None, virtualize_prefix=None, materialize=None)` | Column |

| Parameter | SQL type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `tile` | `TILE` |  | The tile to re-encode. |
| `new_format` | `STRING` |  | A GDAL driver short name that can write a raster, in any case: `'GTiff'`, `'PNG'`, `'COG'`. A name that names none raises. |
{/* END GENERATED gbx_rst_asformat */}

:::note Lightweight tier (pyrx)
Powered by **rasterio**. Output formats are limited to rasterio's bundled-GDAL writable driver set; the tile is re-encoded in the requested format. `'COG'` produces a Cloud Optimized GeoTIFF (512-pixel blocks, size-adaptive ZSTD compression, and overviews when the tile is larger than 512 pixels), the same layout as [`rst_cog_convert`](#rst_cog_convert).
:::

:::note Heavyweight tier
`'COG'` is written by GDAL's COG driver with its default options; its block size, compression and overviews are not the lightweight tier's.
:::

:::note Lightweight Python — virtual-tile force-output
The lightweight **Python** binding accepts three optional keyword arguments the SQL and heavyweight tiers do not — `virtualize_dir`, `virtualize_prefix`, and `materialize` — controlling whether the produced tile carries raster bytes or a bytes-free virtual reference. See [Virtual-tile force-output params](#virtual-tile-overrides).
:::

Convert to another format. A driver that is unknown, or cannot write a raster, raises an error that names it. An error tile in comes out as it is.

### rst_clip

{/* BEGIN GENERATED gbx_rst_clip: edit manifest/functions.json, then run gbx:manifest:gen */}
<Surfaces session python />

| Surface | Call | Returns |
|---|---|---|
| SQL (session) | `gbx_rst_clip(tile, geom, cutline_all_touched, [clip_crs])` | `TILE` |
| Python | `rasterx.functions.rst_clip(tile, geom, cutline_all_touched, clip_crs=None, virtualize_dir=None, virtualize_prefix=None, materialize=None)` | Column |

| Parameter | SQL type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `tile` | `TILE` |  | The tile to clip. |
| `geom` | `BINARY` or `STRING` |  | The cutline: a polygon as WKB, EWKB, WKT or EWKT. |
| `cutline_all_touched` | `BOOLEAN` |  | `true` keeps every pixel the cutline touches; `false` keeps the pixels whose centre it holds. |
| `clip_crs` | `STRING` | `NULL` | The CRS of a cutline that carries no SRID: `EPSG:<code>`, `ESRI:<code>`, WKT or an integer code. The CRS of the tile when NULL. An EWKB or EWKT SRID wins. |
{/* END GENERATED gbx_rst_clip */}

:::note Cutline CRS
A cutline with an EWKB or EWKT SRID is reprojected to the tile's CRS. A plain WKB or WKT cutline takes `clip_crs`, else the tile's CRS. The result is NULL when the cutline misses the tile.
:::

:::note Lightweight Python — virtual-tile force-output
The lightweight **Python** binding accepts three optional keyword arguments the SQL and heavyweight tiers do not — `virtualize_dir`, `virtualize_prefix`, and `materialize` — controlling whether the produced tile carries raster bytes or a bytes-free virtual reference. See [Virtual-tile force-output params](#virtual-tile-overrides).
:::

Clip by geometry. The `clip` argument must be **WKT** (string), **EWKT** (SRID-prefixed string), **WKB** (binary), or **EWKB** (SRID-embedded binary); do not use `st_geomfromtext()` or other DBR native geometry. Optional `clip_crs` (string, source role) declares the CRS of a plain WKB/WKT cutline — an EWKB/EWKT embedded SRID wins, absent → assumed already in the raster CRS. See [Coordinate Reference Systems](./coordinate-reference-systems#source-crs-vs-output-crs).

**CRS handling:**
- **EWKT** (`SRID=4326;POLYGON(...)`) or **EWKB** (SRID encoded in the byte header) — the SRID is read, and if it differs from the raster's CRS the cutline is reprojected before clipping. Use this form whenever the geometry and raster may be in different CRSs.
- **Plain WKT / WKB** (no SRID) — the geometry is assumed to already be in the raster's CRS. If that assumption is wrong (for example, lon/lat polygons against a UTM raster), the cutline will land outside the raster and you'll get an empty or blank output. Either switch to EWKT/EWKB, or reproject the geometry to the raster's CRS first.

_Example clip geometry: a WKT polygon in the raster's native CRS (EPSG:32618, no SRID prefix). To clip with a WGS84 geometry use EWKT: `SRID=4326;POLYGON(...)` — the embedded SRID triggers auto-reprojection to the raster's CRS._

<FunctionExamples
  name="rst_clip"
  sql={rasterxSqlCode}                  sqlSource="docs/tests/python/api/rasterx_functions_sql.py"
  python={rasterxTileopsLightCode}  pythonSource="docs/tests/python/api/rasterx_tileops_python_light.py"
  testFile="docs/tests/python/api/test_rasterx_tileops_python_light.py"

### rst_percentile_stretch

{/* BEGIN GENERATED gbx_rst_percentile_stretch: edit manifest/functions.json, then run gbx:manifest:gen */}
<Surfaces session python scala />

| Surface | Call | Returns |
|---|---|---|
| SQL (session) | `gbx_rst_percentile_stretch(tile, lo_pct, hi_pct)` | `TILE` |
| Python | `rasterx.functions.rst_percentile_stretch(tile, lo_pct, hi_pct, virtualize_dir=None, virtualize_prefix=None, materialize=None)` | Column |

| Parameter | SQL type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `tile` | `TILE` |  | The tile to stretch. |
| `lo_pct` | `DOUBLE` |  | The lower percentile, in `[0, 100]` and below `hi_pct`: the value at it becomes 0. |
| `hi_pct` | `DOUBLE` |  | The upper percentile, in `[0, 100]`: the value at it becomes 255. |
{/* END GENERATED gbx_rst_percentile_stretch */}

:::note Lightweight tier (pyrx)
Powered by **rasterio** + **NumPy**. Computes per-band percentiles over valid pixels, clips to that range, and linearly rescales to [0,255]. Each band is stretched independently.
:::

:::note Lightweight Python — virtual-tile force-output
The lightweight **Python** binding accepts three optional keyword arguments the SQL and heavyweight tiers do not — `virtualize_dir`, `virtualize_prefix`, and `materialize` — controlling whether the produced tile carries raster bytes or a bytes-free virtual reference. See [Virtual-tile force-output params](#virtual-tile-overrides).
:::

Per-band percentile contrast stretch to uint8. For each band, `lo_pct` and `hi_pct` (percentiles in 0–100) define the stretch range. Pixels at the lower percentile map to 0, those at the upper percentile map to 255, and the rest are linearly rescaled. NoData pixels remain 0 in the output. Typical values are `lo_pct=2.0, hi_pct=98.0`, which trim outliers and use the full uint8 range. The output is a uint8 GTiff with the same bands, extent, and CRS as the input.

:::note Lightweight tier (pyrx)
Powered by **rasterio** + **NumPy**. Takes an `ARRAY<tile>` and returns the NoData-aware per-pixel mean; input tiles must share the same grid (shape/extent/CRS). cellid is preserved when all inputs share one, else −1.

:::note Lightweight Python — virtual-tile force-output
The lightweight **Python** binding accepts three optional keyword arguments the SQL and heavyweight tiers do not — `virtualize_dir`, `virtualize_prefix`, and `materialize` — controlling whether the produced tile carries raster bytes or a bytes-free virtual reference. :::

Per-pixel mean across an `ARRAY` of aligned tiles. **On invocation** the tiles are read as a pixel-aligned stack — they must share grid, extent, and CRS, so align them with `rst_align_to` first — and each output pixel is the mean of that location's valid (non-NoData) values across the stack (a pixel that is NoData in *every* input stays NoData). Use for temporal/ensemble composites such as cloud-reduced mean reflectance.

Average multiple tiles (e.g. temporal composite).

|---|---|---|---|

:::note Lightweight tier (pyrx)
Powered by **rasterio** + **NumPy**. Takes an `ARRAY<tile>` and returns a tile where each pixel is the count of valid (non-NoData) inputs across the stack; input tiles must share the same grid (shape/extent/CRS). All-NoData pixels yield NoData in the output (never 0 for an all-NoData pixel). Align tiles with `gbx_rst_align_to` before combining.
:::

Per-pixel count of valid (non-NoData) inputs across an `ARRAY` of aligned tiles. **On invocation** the tiles are read as a pixel-aligned stack — they must share grid, extent, and CRS, so align them with `rst_align_to` first — and each output pixel is the number of inputs whose value at that location is valid; a location that is NoData in *every* input yields NoData (never 0). Use alongside the other `rst_combine*` reducers to build multi-statistic temporal or ensemble composites.

Count of valid pixels per location.

### rst_combinemedian

{/* BEGIN GENERATED gbx_rst_combinemedian: edit manifest/functions.json, then run gbx:manifest:gen */}
<Surfaces session python scala />

| Surface | Call | Returns |
|---|---|---|
| SQL (session) | `gbx_rst_combinemedian(tiles)` | `TILE` |
| Python | `rasterx.functions.rst_combinemedian(tiles, virtualize_dir=None, virtualize_prefix=None, materialize=None)` | Column |

| Parameter | SQL type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `tiles` | `ARRAY<TILE>` |  | The tiles to combine, in an array: aligned tiles that share size, extent and CRS (use `gbx_rst_align_to` first). |
{/* END GENERATED gbx_rst_combinemedian */}

:::note Lightweight tier (pyrx)
Powered by **rasterio** + **NumPy**. Takes an `ARRAY<tile>` and returns the NoData-aware per-pixel median; input tiles must share the same grid (shape/extent/CRS). Even-count median uses the mean of the two middle values (`numpy np.ma.median` convention). All-NoData pixels yield NoData in the output. Align tiles with `gbx_rst_align_to` before combining.
:::

Per-pixel median across an `ARRAY` of aligned tiles. **On invocation** the tiles are read as a pixel-aligned stack — they must share grid, extent, and CRS, so align them with `rst_align_to` first — and each output pixel is the median of that location's valid (non-NoData) values across the stack (even counts average the two middle values; a pixel that is NoData in *every* input stays NoData). Robust to outliers — useful for seasonal median composites from time-series imagery.

Median pixel value per location.

{/* BEGIN GENERATED gbx_rst_convolve: edit manifest/functions.json, then run gbx:manifest:gen */}
<Surfaces session python scala />

| Surface | Call | Returns |
|---|---|---|
| SQL (session) | `gbx_rst_convolve(tile, kernel)` | `TILE` |
| Python | `rasterx.functions.rst_convolve(tile, kernel, virtualize_dir=None, virtualize_prefix=None, materialize=None)` | Column |

| Parameter | SQL type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `tile` | `TILE` |  | The tile to convolve. |
| `kernel` | `ARRAY<ARRAY<DOUBLE>>` |  | A 2-D array of weights with an odd number of rows and columns, applied unflipped (correlation). |
{/* END GENERATED gbx_rst_convolve */}

See [interpolation & fill](./grid-raster-tessellation#interpolation-and-fill) to choose among sampling, resampling, and gap-filling.

:::note Lightweight tier (pyrx)
Powered by **SciPy** (`scipy.ndimage`). Both tiers give a Float64 result and apply the kernel unflipped, with the edges zero-padded. A NoData or masked neighbour adds 0, and the result is never NoData.
:::

:::note Lightweight Python — virtual-tile force-output
The lightweight **Python** binding accepts three optional keyword arguments the SQL and heavyweight tiers do not — `virtualize_dir`, `virtualize_prefix`, and `materialize` — controlling whether the produced tile carries raster bytes or a bytes-free virtual reference. See [Virtual-tile force-output params](#virtual-tile-overrides).
:::

Apply a convolution kernel. A kernel that is not a rectangular array with an odd number of rows and columns raises an error that names its shape.

_Example kernel: 3×3 identity (`[[0,0,0],[0,1,0],[0,0,0]]`), which passes pixels through unchanged._

| Python | `rasterx.functions.rst_mapalgebra(tiles, expression, virtualize_dir=None, virtualize_prefix=None, materialize=None)` | Column |

| Parameter | SQL type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `tiles` | `ARRAY<TILE>` |  | The input tiles. Band 1 of each tile binds to `A`, `B`, `C` and so on, in array order. |
| `expression` | `STRING` |  | The expression: a JSON envelope with a `calc` key, or a bare expression such as `'(A - B) / (A + B)'`. |
{/* END GENERATED gbx_rst_mapalgebra */}

:::note Lightweight tier (pyrx)
Powered by **NumExpr**. Bands map to `A`, `B`, `C`, … and the `calc` expression is evaluated with NumExpr (no `gdal_calc` NumPy builtins); single-band Float32 output.
:::

:::note Lightweight Python — virtual-tile force-output
The lightweight **Python** binding accepts three optional keyword arguments the SQL and heavyweight tiers do not — `virtualize_dir`, `virtualize_prefix`, and `materialize` — controlling whether the produced tile carries raster bytes or a bytes-free virtual reference. See [Virtual-tile force-output params](#virtual-tile-overrides).
:::

Map algebra expression (e.g. `A - B`).

**How the inputs bind.** `tiles` is an `ARRAY<tile>`. By default, band 1 of each tile, **in array order**, binds to the variables `A`, `B`, `C`, … (`A` = first tile, `B` = second, …). The result is a single-band Float32 tile on the first input's georeference.

**Spec format (same on both tiers).** The expression is accepted in **either** of two forms — identical across SQL, Scala, and both Python tiers:

- **JSON envelope** (recommended; the `gdal_calc` spec shape): a JSON object with a `calc` expression — `'{"calc": "A * 2"}'`.
- **Bare expression string**: the calc expression on its own — `"A * 2"`.


**Selecting a specific band or raster per variable (`A_index` / `A_band`).** You do **not** need to decompose a multiband raster to do band math. The JSON envelope's per-variable keys — `A_index` / `A_band` (and `B_`, `C_`, …) — map each variable to a chosen **raster** (0-based, into the `tiles` array) and a chosen **1-based band**, mirroring `gdal_calc`. For example, **NDVI from bands 4 (NIR) and 3 (Red) of a single raster**:

```json
{"calc": "(A - B) / (A + B)", "A_index": 0, "B_index": 0, "A_band": 4, "B_band": 3}
```

Both `A` and `B` read raster `0` (the one tile in the array), with `A` = band 4 and `B` = band 3 — the direct equivalent of `gdal_calc -A in.tif --A_band=4 -B in.tif --B_band=3 --calc="(A - B) / (A + B)"`. A variable with no `*_index`/`*_band` keeps the default (its ordinal raster, band 1). **This works on both tiers.**

The only `gdal_calc` envelope key the lightweight tier does **not** support is `extra_options` (raw CLI flags with no NumExpr equivalent); it raises a clear error rather than dropping it silently. The heavy tier honors `extra_options` as well.

:::caution Expression language differs by engine
The **envelope** is portable, but the **calc expression language is not fully portable** because each tier uses a different evaluator: the heavy tier shells out to GDAL's `gdal_calc` (NumPy expression syntax); the lightweight tier evaluates with [NumExpr](https://numexpr.readthedocs.io/). They agree on ordinary arithmetic and comparisons (`A * 2`, `(A - B) / (A + B)`, `A > 0`), so those expressions run **unchanged on both tiers**. They diverge on function spellings — e.g. `gdal_calc` accepts NumPy calls like `numpy.where(A > 0, A, 0)`, whereas NumExpr wants `where(A > 0, A, 0)`. Keep expressions to basic arithmetic for cross-tier portability. For the heavy tier's full syntax and options, see the GDAL raster calculator reference: [gdal_raster_calc](https://gdal.org/en/stable/programs/gdal_raster_calc.html#gdal-raster-calc).
:::

<FunctionExamples
  name="rst_mapalgebra"
  sql={rasterxSqlCode}                  sqlSource="docs/tests/python/api/rasterx_functions_sql.py"
  python={rasterxBandmathLightCode} pythonSource="docs/tests/python/api/rasterx_bandmath_python_light.py"
  testFile="docs/tests/python/api/test_rasterx_bandmath_python_light.py"
/>

### rst_merge

{/* BEGIN GENERATED gbx_rst_merge: edit manifest/functions.json, then run gbx:manifest:gen */}
<Surfaces session python scala />

| Surface | Call | Returns |
|---|---|---|
| SQL (session) | `gbx_rst_merge(tiles)` | `TILE` |
| Python | `rasterx.functions.rst_merge(tiles, virtualize_dir=None, virtualize_prefix=None, materialize=None)` | Column |

| Parameter | SQL type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `tiles` | `ARRAY<TILE>` |  | The tiles to mosaic, in an array. Tiles in another CRS are reprojected onto the grid of a tile in the reference CRS. |
{/* END GENERATED gbx_rst_merge */}

:::note Lightweight tier (pyrx)
Powered by **rasterio** (`rasterio.merge`). Takes an `ARRAY<tile>` (in one row) and mosaics them into a single tile spanning the union extent. Where tiles overlap, the winner does not depend on the array order: the tile whose bytes sort last wins. Tiles in a different CRS are reprojected onto the pixel size and grid of a tile in the reference CRS and mosaicked with it. A tile reprojected from another CRS that declares no NoData (or has its own mask band) gets the footprint of the warp as its mask. The mosaic then has a mask band, and a pixel no tile reaches is invalid in it. A gap between tiles that are all in the reference CRS and declare no NoData is still filled with zeros. The JVM level (L1) does the same. It reprojects a tile in another CRS onto the pixel size and grid of a tile in the reference CRS, the smallest EPSG code present. It then mosaics them and never leaves a tile out.
:::

:::note Lightweight Python — virtual-tile force-output
The lightweight **Python** binding accepts three optional keyword arguments the SQL and heavyweight tiers do not — `virtualize_dir`, `virtualize_prefix`, and `materialize` — controlling whether the produced tile carries raster bytes or a bytes-free virtual reference. See [Virtual-tile force-output params](#virtual-tile-overrides).
:::

Merge tiles into mosaic.

### rst_resample

{/* BEGIN GENERATED gbx_rst_resample: edit manifest/functions.json, then run gbx:manifest:gen */}
<Surfaces session python scala />

| Surface | Call | Returns |
|---|---|---|
| SQL (session) | `gbx_rst_resample(tile, factor, [algorithm])` | `TILE` |
| Python | `rasterx.functions.rst_resample(tile, factor, algorithm=None, virtualize_dir=None, virtualize_prefix=None, materialize=None)` | Column |

| Parameter | SQL type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `tile` | `TILE` |  | The tile to resample. |
| `factor` | `DOUBLE` |  | The multiplicative factor, above 0: above 1 upsamples, below 1 downsamples. |
| `algorithm` | `STRING` | `'bilinear'` | The resampling algorithm: `near`, `bilinear`, `cubic`, `cubicspline`, `lanczos`, `average`, `mode`, `max`, `min`, `med`, `q1` or `q3` (any case; `nearest` is accepted as `near`). A word that names none raises. |
{/* END GENERATED gbx_rst_resample */}

See [interpolation & fill](./grid-raster-tessellation#interpolation-and-fill) to choose among sampling, resampling, and gap-filling.

:::note Lightweight tier (pyrx)
Powered by **rasterio** (`rasterio.warp`).
:::

Resample a raster tile by a multiplicative factor via `gdal.Warp -r`, scaling pixel dimensions up or down relative to the source. A factor of 0 or less, or an unknown algorithm, raises an error that names the value.

:::note Lightweight Python — virtual-tile force-output
The lightweight **Python** binding accepts three optional keyword arguments the SQL and heavyweight tiers do not — `virtualize_dir`, `virtualize_prefix`, and `materialize` — controlling whether the produced tile carries raster bytes or a bytes-free virtual reference. See [Virtual-tile force-output params](#virtual-tile-overrides).
:::

:::tip Esri Resample / changing cell size
The `rst_resample*` family is GeoBrix's equivalent of the ArcGIS **Resample** tool — producing a *new* raster at a different cell size with a chosen resampling method. The `algorithm` is one of `near`, `bilinear`, `cubic`, `cubicspline`, `lanczos`, `average`, `mode`, `max`, `min`, `med`, `q1` or `q3`, in any case. Pick the variant by how you specify the target:

- **`rst_resample`** — by a multiplicative **factor** (e.g. `2.0` doubles the grid).
- **[`rst_resample_to_res`](#rst_resample_to_res)** — by target **ground resolution** in CRS units (e.g. metres per pixel).
- **[`rst_resample_to_size`](#rst_resample_to_size)** — by target **pixel dimensions** (width × height).

Unlike [`rst_sample`](#rst_sample) — which reads a value at a point and is nearest-pixel only — resampling rewrites the whole grid. So to *sample* with **bilinear or cubic interpolation**, resample first with the desired `algorithm`, then call `rst_sample`.

See [Raster Sampling](./raster-sampling) for the full workflow guide.
:::

:::note Lightweight tier (pyrx)
Powered by **rasterio** (`rasterio.warp`) + **pyproj**. Reprojection uses the GDAL build bundled with rasterio, whose projection database and driver set may be narrower than the heavyweight tier.
:::

Reproject to a target CRS given as a *string* (`EPSG:x` / `ESRI:x` / WKT / PROJ4; an int-castable string is treated as a SRID). See [Coordinate Reference Systems](./coordinate-reference-systems).

Distinct from `rst_transform` (integer EPSG only): `rst_transformcrs` accepts any CRS string — an authority code (`EPSG:3857`, `ESRI:54008`), WKT, or PROJ4 — so you can reproject to a non-EPSG target. An int-castable string (`'3857'`) is treated as an EPSG SRID. See the [CRS: SRID int vs CRS string](#rst_crs) note above.

### rst_tilexyz

{/* BEGIN GENERATED gbx_rst_tilexyz: edit manifest/functions.json, then run gbx:manifest:gen */}
<Surfaces session python scala />

| Surface | Call | Returns |
|---|---|---|
| SQL (session) | `gbx_rst_tilexyz(tile, z, x, y, [format], [size], [resampling], [rescale])` | `BINARY` |
| Python | `rasterx.functions.rst_tilexyz(tile, z, x, y, format=None, size=None, resampling=None, rescale=None)` | Column |

| Parameter | SQL type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `tile` | `TILE` |  | The tile to render. |
| `z` | `INT` |  | The zoom level of the web tile, from 0. |
| `x` | `INT` |  | The tile column, from 0 to 2^z - 1. |
| `y` | `INT` |  | The tile row (north-down slippy-map order), from 0 to 2^z - 1. |
| `format` | `STRING` | `'PNG'` | `'PNG'`, `'JPEG'` or `'WEBP'`, in any case. Any other word raises. |
| `size` | `INT` | `256` | The side of the output tile in pixels, in (0, 4096]. Any other value raises. |
| `resampling` | `STRING` | `'bilinear'` | The GDAL warp resampling: `near`, `bilinear`, `cubic`, `cubicspline`, `lanczos`, `average`, `mode`, `max`, `min`, `med`, `q1` or `q3`. Any other word raises. |
| `rescale` | `STRING` | `'auto'` | The 8-bit contrast: `'auto'` rescales a tile that is not 8-bit by its band minimum and maximum and passes an 8-bit one through, `'none'` keeps the full range of the data type, and `'min,max'` rescales between two numbers. Any other text raises. |
{/* END GENERATED gbx_rst_tilexyz */}

:::note Lightweight tier (pyrx)
Powered by **rio-tiler** + **morecantile**. Out-of-extent tiles return a transparent PNG (never null); available output formats depend on rasterio's bundled GDAL build.
:::

Render a single web-mercator XYZ tile from a raster as encoded image bytes (e.g. PNG, JPEG, WebP) at the given tile coordinates and pixel size.

A bad argument raises an error that names the value. Bad arguments are a `format` other than `PNG`, `JPEG` or `WEBP`, a `size` outside (0, 4096], a `resampling` word that names none, or a `rescale` that is not `auto`, `none` or `min,max`. It raises also when the tile is NULL or cannot be read, because the argument is checked first; a NULL `z`, `x` or `y` gives NULL.

:::note Display RGB(A) output (both tiers)
The output is a display web-map tile, not the source's raw bands. **PNG and WebP** are **RGBA** (4-band); **JPEG** is **RGB** (3-band, no alpha). The alpha channel is a binary transparency mask derived from the source's valid-data footprint, so a pixel that is NoData — whether outside the raster or an internal hole — renders **transparent**, and both the lightweight and heavyweight tiers agree on which pixels are transparent. Band mapping matches the lightweight tier: a single-band source becomes greyscale RGB (`R=G=B`), a two-band source uses band 1 as greyscale RGB and band 2 as the alpha channel, three bands map to R, G, B, an existing fourth band is treated as alpha, and five-or-more-band sources use the first three bands as RGB. Non-8-bit sources are contrast-rescaled to 8-bit per the `rescale` argument (default `"auto"`; see the `rescale` note above). WebP alpha requires GDAL WebP-alpha support in the runtime; where absent, WebP falls back to RGB.
:::

### rst_to_webmercator

{/* BEGIN GENERATED gbx_rst_to_webmercator: edit manifest/functions.json, then run gbx:manifest:gen */}
<Surfaces session python scala />

| Surface | Call | Returns |
|---|---|---|
| SQL (session) | `gbx_rst_to_webmercator(tile, [resampling])` | `TILE` |
| Python | `rasterx.functions.rst_to_webmercator(tile, resampling=None, virtualize_dir=None, virtualize_prefix=None, materialize=None)` | Column |

| Parameter | SQL type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `tile` | `TILE` |  | The tile to reproject. |
| `resampling` | `STRING` | `'bilinear'` | The resampling algorithm: `near`, `bilinear`, `cubic`, `cubicspline`, `lanczos`, `average`, `mode`, `max`, `min`, `med`, `q1` or `q3` (any case; `nearest` is accepted as `near`). A word that names none raises. |
{/* END GENERATED gbx_rst_to_webmercator */}

:::note Lightweight tier (pyrx)
Powered by **rasterio** (`rasterio.warp`). Uses rasterio's bundled GDAL build, whose projection and driver coverage may be narrower than the heavyweight tier.
:::

:::note Lightweight Python — virtual-tile force-output
The lightweight **Python** binding accepts three optional keyword arguments the SQL and heavyweight tiers do not — `virtualize_dir`, `virtualize_prefix`, and `materialize` — controlling whether the produced tile carries raster bytes or a bytes-free virtual reference. See [Virtual-tile force-output params](#virtual-tile-overrides).
:::

Reproject a raster to EPSG:3857 (Web Mercator) using bilinear resampling by default. The returned tile carries `srid = 3857`. A resampling word that names none raises an error that names it. A tile with no CRS gives an error tile.

{/* BEGIN GENERATED gbx_rst_xyzpyramid: edit manifest/functions.json, then run gbx:manifest:gen */}
<Surfaces session python scala />

| Surface | Call | Returns |
|---|---|---|
| SQL (session) | `gbx_rst_xyzpyramid(tile, min_z, max_z, [format], [size], [resampling], [rescale])` | rows |
| Python | `rasterx.functions.rst_xyzpyramid(df, tile, min_z, max_z, format=None, size=None, resampling=None, rescale=None)` | DataFrame |

Rows: `z` `INT`, `x` `INT`, `y` `INT`, `bytes` `BINARY`.

| Parameter | SQL type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `tile` | `TILE` |  | The tile to render. |
| `min_z` | `INT` |  | The first zoom level, from 0. |
| `max_z` | `INT` |  | The last zoom level, at least `min_z` and at most 20. |
| `format` | `STRING` | `'PNG'` | `'PNG'`, `'JPEG'` or `'WEBP'`, in any case. Any other word raises. |
| `size` | `INT` | `256` | The side of each output tile in pixels, in (0, 4096]. Any other value raises. |
| `resampling` | `STRING` | `'bilinear'` | The GDAL warp resampling, as for `gbx_rst_tilexyz`. Any other word raises. |
| `rescale` | `STRING` | `'auto'` | The 8-bit contrast, as for `gbx_rst_tilexyz`. |
{/* END GENERATED gbx_rst_xyzpyramid */}

<Impl udtf/>

:::note Lightweight tier (pyrx)
Powered by **rio-tiler** + **morecantile**. Streams one XYZ tile row per intersecting tile via streaming UDTF. Bounded by `max_z <= 20` and at most 1,000,000 candidate tiles.
:::

Generator: explode a raster into one row per intersecting `(z, x, y)` tile across a zoom range, producing PNG bytes per tile. Use `LATERAL VIEW` to materialize the rows; the output struct exposes `z`, `x`, `y`, and `bytes`. Each tile is rendered by `rst_tilexyz`, so the per-tile PNG is display **RGBA** with the same band mapping and binary NoData alpha described above.

## Vector↔raster bridge {#vector-bridge}

Move data between the raster (`tile`) and vector (`geom`) worlds.

### rst_polygonize

{/* BEGIN GENERATED gbx_rst_polygonize: edit manifest/functions.json, then run gbx:manifest:gen */}
<Surfaces session python />

| Surface | Call | Returns |
|---|---|---|
| SQL (session) | `gbx_rst_polygonize(tile, [band], [connectedness])` | rows |
| Python | `rasterx.functions.rst_polygonize(df, tile, band=None, connectedness=None)` | DataFrame |

Rows: `geom_wkb` `BINARY`, `value` `DOUBLE`.

| Parameter | SQL type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `tile` | `TILE` |  | The tile to polygonize. |
| `band` | `INT` | `1` | The band to read, from 1. |
| `connectedness` | `INT` | `4` | `4` joins pixels that share an edge; `8` also joins pixels that touch at a corner. |
{/* END GENERATED gbx_rst_polygonize */}

<Impl udtf/>

:::note Lightweight tier (pyrx)
Powered by **rasterio** (`rasterio.features`).
:::

`gbx_rst_polygonize` is a table function: call it with `LATERAL`. It streams one row per region, so a raster with many regions does not buffer an array. The manager serves this form at every level; the JVM class that returns an array from a scalar call is not selected.

```sql
SELECT t.geom_wkb, t.value FROM <table>, LATERAL gbx_rst_polygonize(tile, band, connectedness) t
```

### rst_rasterize

{/* BEGIN GENERATED gbx_rst_rasterize: edit manifest/functions.json, then run gbx:manifest:gen */}
<Surfaces session python />

| Surface | Call | Returns |
|---|---|---|
| SQL (session) | `gbx_rst_rasterize(geom, value, xmin, ymin, xmax, ymax, width_px, height_px, [out_srid], [out_crs])` | `TILE` |
| Python | `rasterx.functions.rst_rasterize(geom, value, xmin, ymin, xmax, ymax, width_px, height_px, out_srid=None, out_crs=None)` | Column |

| Parameter | SQL type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `geom` | `BINARY` or `STRING` |  | The geometry: WKB, EWKB, WKT or EWKT. A geometry with an SRID is reprojected into the output CRS first. |
| `value` | `DOUBLE` |  | The value burned into the pixels the geometry covers. |
| `xmin` | `DOUBLE` |  | The west edge of the output in the output CRS. |
| `ymin` | `DOUBLE` |  | The south edge of the output. |
| `xmax` | `DOUBLE` |  | The east edge of the output, above `xmin`. |
| `ymax` | `DOUBLE` |  | The north edge of the output, above `ymin`. |
| `width_px` | `INT` |  | The output width in pixels, above 0. |
| `height_px` | `INT` |  | The output height in pixels, above 0. |
| `out_srid` | `INT` | `NULL` | The EPSG or ESRI code of the output CRS. NULL leaves the output without a CRS, or with the geometry's own when `out_crs` is NULL too. |
| `out_crs` | `STRING` | `NULL` | The output CRS as `EPSG:<n>`, `ESRI:<n>` or WKT. When set it wins over `out_srid`; setting both raises. |
{/* END GENERATED gbx_rst_rasterize */}

:::note Lightweight tier (pyrx)
Powered by **rasterio** (`rasterio.features`).
:::

:::note Lightweight-only `out_crs` parameter
The lightweight **Python** binding accepts an optional trailing `out_crs` (string CRS) argument that the SQL and heavyweight/Scala tiers do not — the heavyweight `RST_Rasterize` builder is strictly 9-argument (`out_srid` only) and rejects a 10th argument. In lightweight Python, `out_crs` (string) wins over the int `out_srid`; both → error; neither → the geometry's carried source CRS. This is a lightweight superset, not a heavyweight regression.
:::

Burn a polygon (WKB) into a fresh GeoTIFF tile at the given extent and pixel dimensions. Pixels inside the polygon carry `burnValue`; pixels outside are NoData. The output CRS is `out_srid` (integer EPSG code); the geometry is reprojected from its source CRS into the output CRS before burning. When `out_srid` is omitted the geometry's carried source CRS is used. See [Coordinate Reference Systems](./coordinate-reference-systems#source-crs-vs-output-crs).

**SQL:**

## Terrain Analysis {#terrain}

Thin wrappers around `gdal.DEMProcessing` for digital elevation model (DEM) derivatives. Each function takes a single-band DEM tile and returns a derived tile of the same footprint. See the [Helios notebooks](../notebooks/helios) for a worked example: `gbx_rst_slope`, `gbx_rst_aspect`, and `gbx_rst_hillshade` are applied to 3DEP DEMs in NB03 to produce terrain layers and a per-H3-cell solar score.

### rst_aspect

{/* BEGIN GENERATED gbx_rst_aspect: edit manifest/functions.json, then run gbx:manifest:gen */}
<Surfaces session python scala />

| Surface | Call | Returns |
|---|---|---|
| SQL (session) | `gbx_rst_aspect(tile, [trigonometric], [zero_for_flat])` | `TILE` |
| Python | `rasterx.functions.rst_aspect(tile, trigonometric=None, zero_for_flat=None, virtualize_dir=None, virtualize_prefix=None, materialize=None)` | Column |

| Parameter | SQL type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `tile` | `TILE` |  | The single-band DEM tile. |
| `trigonometric` | `BOOLEAN` | `false` | `true` gives counter-clockwise degrees from east; `false` gives compass degrees clockwise from north. |
| `zero_for_flat` | `BOOLEAN` | `false` | `true` gives 0 for flat cells; `false` leaves them NoData. |
{/* END GENERATED gbx_rst_aspect */}

:::note Edges and NoData
A pixel whose 3×3 window holds NoData, and the one-pixel border, are NoData. Values can differ from GDAL's `gdaldem` in the last digits.
:::

:::note Lightweight Python — virtual-tile force-output
The Python function accepts three optional keyword arguments that SQL does not: `virtualize_dir`, `virtualize_prefix` and `materialize`. They choose whether the tile carries raster bytes or a bytes-free virtual reference. See [Virtual-tile force-output params](#virtual-tile-overrides).
:::

Compass direction of steepest descent in degrees (0=N, 90=E, 180=S, 270=W). Flat areas are NoData unless `zero_for_flat` is true. Set `trigonometric` to true for the mathematical convention (0=E, counter-clockwise).

With `zero_for_flat`, GDAL drops the NoData value, so its border pixels are 0. The NumPy kernel keeps them NoData.

<FunctionExamples
  name="rst_aspect"
  sql={rasterxSqlCode}                  sqlSource="docs/tests/python/api/rasterx_functions_sql.py"
  python={rasterxTerrainLightCode} pythonSource="docs/tests/python/api/rasterx_terrain_python_light.py"
  testFile="docs/tests/python/api/test_rasterx_terrain_python_light.py"
/>

### rst_color_relief

{/* BEGIN GENERATED gbx_rst_color_relief: edit manifest/functions.json, then run gbx:manifest:gen */}
<Surfaces session python scala />

| Surface | Call | Returns |
|---|---|---|
| SQL (session) | `gbx_rst_color_relief(tile, color_table_path)` | `TILE` |
| Python | `rasterx.functions.rst_color_relief(tile, color_table_path, virtualize_dir=None, virtualize_prefix=None, materialize=None)` | Column |

| Parameter | SQL type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `tile` | `TILE` |  | The single-band DEM tile. |
| `color_table_path` | `STRING` |  | The path of a `gdaldem` colour file: one `elevation R G B [A]` line per stop, with `nv` for NoData and `N%` for a share of the value range. The file must exist. |
{/* END GENERATED gbx_rst_color_relief */}

:::note Cross-tier output differs (both tiers, by design)
The two tiers use **different color interpolation engines**, so their pixel values are close but **not identical** and are not byte-comparable: the heavyweight tier calls GDAL `gdal.DEMProcessing` color-relief (GDAL's native C interpolation), while the lightweight (`pyrx`) tier is a **NumPy** reimplementation using per-channel `np.interp`. Two known differences on the lightweight tier: the `gdaldem` `default` color keyword is **not supported** (it is skipped; out-of-range elevations are clamped to the nearest color stop by `np.interp` instead), and boundary/edge interpolation may differ slightly. Both tiers emit an RGB or RGBA Byte tile (RGBA when any color-table entry carries an alpha column). Because the outputs diverge, this function is not cross-tier fingerprint-compared in benchmarks (measured `timing-only`); pick the tier by your pipeline (heavyweight for `gdaldem`-exact output, lightweight for the pure-Python/Serverless path).
:::

:::note Lightweight Python — virtual-tile force-output
The lightweight **Python** binding accepts three optional keyword arguments the SQL and heavyweight tiers do not — `virtualize_dir`, `virtualize_prefix`, and `materialize` — controlling whether the produced tile carries raster bytes or a bytes-free virtual reference. See [Virtual-tile force-output params](#virtual-tile-overrides).
:::

Apply a `gdaldem` color table (`elevation R G B [A]` per line) to produce an RGB(A) visualization tile. Special values `nv` (NoData color), `0%`, and `100%` (percentages of the band value range) are honored on both tiers. The `default` keyword is honored only on the heavyweight tier (see the tier note above).

  scala={scalaApiExamplesCode}          scalaSource="docs/tests/scala/api/ScalaApiExamples.scala"
  testFile="docs/tests/python/api/test_rasterx_terrain_python_light.py"
/>

### rst_hillshade

{/* BEGIN GENERATED gbx_rst_hillshade: edit manifest/functions.json, then run gbx:manifest:gen */}
<Surfaces session python />

| Surface | Call | Returns |
|---|---|---|
| SQL (session) | `gbx_rst_hillshade(tile, [azimuth], [altitude], [z_factor], [xscale], [yscale])` | `TILE` |
| Python | `rasterx.functions.rst_hillshade(tile, azimuth=None, altitude=None, z_factor=None, xscale=None, yscale=None, virtualize_dir=None, virtualize_prefix=None, materialize=None)` | Column |

| Parameter | SQL type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `tile` | `TILE` |  | The single-band DEM tile. |
| `azimuth` | `DOUBLE` | `315.0` | Sun azimuth in degrees clockwise from north. |
| `altitude` | `DOUBLE` | `45.0` | Sun elevation above the horizon in degrees. |
| `z_factor` | `DOUBLE` | `1.0` | Vertical exaggeration applied to the gradients. |
| `xscale` | `DOUBLE` | `NULL` | Vertical units per horizontal unit along x; set it with `yscale`. When both are NULL, the scale comes from the raster CRS. |
| `yscale` | `DOUBLE` | `NULL` | Vertical units per horizontal unit along y; set it with `xscale`. |
{/* END GENERATED gbx_rst_hillshade */}

:::note Edges and NoData
A pixel whose 3×3 window holds NoData, and the one-pixel border, are NoData. Values can differ from GDAL's `gdaldem` in the last digits.
:::

:::note Lightweight Python — virtual-tile force-output
The Python function accepts three optional keyword arguments that SQL does not: `virtualize_dir`, `virtualize_prefix` and `materialize`. They choose whether the tile carries raster bytes or a bytes-free virtual reference. See [Virtual-tile force-output params](#virtual-tile-overrides).
:::

Shaded relief as an 8-bit image with values from 1 to 255; 0 is NoData. Common values are a north-west sun at azimuth `315.0`, altitude `45.0` and `z_factor` 1.0. Pass both `xscale` and `yscale`, or neither.

### rst_slope

{/* BEGIN GENERATED gbx_rst_slope: edit manifest/functions.json, then run gbx:manifest:gen */}
<Surfaces session python />

| Surface | Call | Returns |
|---|---|---|
| SQL (session) | `gbx_rst_slope(tile, [unit], [xscale], [yscale])` | `TILE` |
| Python | `rasterx.functions.rst_slope(tile, unit=None, xscale=None, yscale=None, virtualize_dir=None, virtualize_prefix=None, materialize=None)` | Column |

| Parameter | SQL type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `tile` | `TILE` |  | The single-band DEM tile. |
| `unit` | `STRING` | `'degrees'` | `'degrees'` or `'percent'`. |
| `xscale` | `DOUBLE` | `NULL` | Vertical units per horizontal unit along x; set it with `yscale`. When both are NULL, the scale comes from the raster CRS. |
| `yscale` | `DOUBLE` | `NULL` | Vertical units per horizontal unit along y; set it with `xscale`. |
{/* END GENERATED gbx_rst_slope */}

:::note Lightweight tier (pyrx)
Powered by **NumPy** — a reimplementation of GDAL `gdaldem`. Results are close but not bit-identical: edge pixels are filled by replicating the border (gdaldem leaves them NoData) and NoData cells are not excluded from the 3×3 window.
:::

:::note Lightweight Python — virtual-tile force-output
The lightweight **Python** binding accepts three optional keyword arguments the SQL and heavyweight tiers do not — `virtualize_dir`, `virtualize_prefix`, and `materialize` — controlling whether the produced tile carries raster bytes or a bytes-free virtual reference. See [Virtual-tile force-output params](#virtual-tile-overrides).
:::

Compute slope per pixel. `unit` is `'degrees'` or `'percent'`; `xscale` and `yscale` are the elevation/horizontal unit ratio per axis (supply both or neither; when omitted, GDAL 3.11+ auto-derives from the raster CRS). For isotropic scaling pass the same value to both (e.g. `1.0, 1.0` for a projected CRS in metres).

## Spectral Indices

Multi-band satellite math. Band arguments are 1-based GDAL band indices; the output is always a single-band Float32 GeoTIFF tile. `gbx_rst_ndvi` is documented under [Operations](#rst_ndvi).

### rst_evi

{/* BEGIN GENERATED gbx_rst_evi: edit manifest/functions.json, then run gbx:manifest:gen */}
<Surfaces session python />

| Surface | Call | Returns |
|---|---|---|
| SQL (session) | `gbx_rst_evi(tile, red_idx, nir_idx, blue_idx, [l], [c1], [c2], [g])` | `TILE` |
| Python | `rasterx.functions.rst_evi(tile, red_idx, nir_idx, blue_idx, l=None, c1=None, c2=None, g=None, virtualize_dir=None, virtualize_prefix=None, materialize=None)` | Column |

| Parameter | SQL type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `tile` | `TILE` |  | The multi-band tile. |
| `red_idx` | `INT` |  | The 1-based band of red. |
| `nir_idx` | `INT` |  | The 1-based band of near infrared. |
| `blue_idx` | `INT` |  | The 1-based band of blue. |
| `l` | `DOUBLE` | `1.0` | The canopy background adjustment. |
| `c1` | `DOUBLE` | `6.0` | The aerosol coefficient for red. |
| `c2` | `DOUBLE` | `7.5` | The aerosol coefficient for blue. |
| `g` | `DOUBLE` | `2.5` | The gain factor. |
{/* END GENERATED gbx_rst_evi */}

:::note NoData
A pixel where an input band is NoData, or where the denominator is 0, is NoData (-9999).
:::

:::note Lightweight Python — virtual-tile force-output
The Python function accepts three optional keyword arguments that SQL does not: `virtualize_dir`, `virtualize_prefix` and `materialize`. They choose whether the tile carries raster bytes or a bytes-free virtual reference. See [Virtual-tile force-output params](#virtual-tile-overrides).
:::

Enhanced Vegetation Index. Formula: `G * (NIR - Red) / (NIR + C1*Red - C2*Blue + L)` with MODIS canonical coefficients `G=2.5, L=1.0, C1=6.0, C2=7.5`.

/>

### rst_land_cover

{/* BEGIN GENERATED gbx_rst_land_cover: edit manifest/functions.json, then run gbx:manifest:gen */}
<Surfaces session python />

| Surface | Call | Returns |
|---|---|---|
| SQL (session) | `gbx_rst_land_cover(tile, [method], [n_clusters], [smooth])` | `TILE` |
| Python | `rasterx.functions.rst_land_cover(tile, method=None, n_clusters=None, smooth=None, virtualize_dir=None, virtualize_prefix=None, materialize=None)` | Column |

| Parameter | SQL type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `tile` | `TILE` |  | A tile of 3 or more bands: red, green, blue (and optionally near-infrared). |
| `method` | `STRING` | `'hybrid'` | `'spectral'` (thresholds on excess green and brightness), `'kmeans'` or `'hybrid'` (a k-means partition labelled by the same thresholds), in any case. Another word raises. |
| `n_clusters` | `INT` | `6` | The k-means cluster count (`'kmeans'` and `'hybrid'`), 1 or more. |
| `smooth` | `INT` | `3` | The de-speckle window in pixels: isolated specks of a class are dropped; 0 turns it off. |
{/* END GENERATED gbx_rst_land_cover */}

:::note Lightweight tier (pyrx)
Powered by **rasterio** + **NumPy** + **SciPy**. Classifies a multi-band RGB(+NIR) tile into land-cover class ids using spectral thresholds, k-means clustering, or a hybrid approach. Registered as `gbx_rst_land_cover` in SQL (light-only; no heavyweight Scala equivalent).
:::

:::note Lightweight Python — virtual-tile force-output
The lightweight **Python** binding accepts three optional keyword arguments the SQL tier does not — `virtualize_dir`, `virtualize_prefix`, and `materialize` — controlling whether the produced tile carries raster bytes or a bytes-free virtual reference. See [Virtual-tile force-output params](#virtual-tile-overrides).
:::

Classify an RGB(+NIR) tile into land-cover class ids. The `'spectral'` method uses fixed excess-green and brightness thresholds. The `'kmeans'` method partitions the pixels with a `scipy` k-means over RGB, excess green and brightness, then labels each cluster by the same thresholds. The default `'hybrid'` is the same partition as `'kmeans'` today, kept as its own word so a later release can diverge it. `smooth` runs a binary-opening pass that drops isolated salt-and-pepper specks of each class back to NoData.

Requires a **3+ band** tile (RGB, optionally +NIR — a 4th band is read but not yet consumed by
any classifier). A tile with fewer bands is an error tile. Each output pixel is an index into the class scheme —
the class list `["vegetation", "bare", "impervious", "dark"]` by default — so
map id to name by indexing that list with the value. NoData / unclassified pixels are `-1`, which is
also the output tile's NoData value.

:::tip Tracing class regions to polygons, with a metric min-area filter
Because the output is an ordinary single-band tile, trace its class regions to polygons the
same way as any other `rst_*` result — via the registered
[`gbx_rst_polygonize(tile, band, connectedness)`](#rst_polygonize) streaming UDTF.
`rst_polygonize` has no `min_area` parameter, so filter out small regions **after**
polygonizing, in metric square meters, via `core.crs.area_m2(geom, crs)` — it takes the
geodesic area on the CRS's own ellipsoid for a geographic CRS (e.g. EPSG:4326), instead of a
raw `geom.area` in square degrees that would silently drop every region against a metric
threshold.
:::

  python={pyrxCode}                pythonSource="docs/tests/python/api/pyrx_functions.py"
  testFile="python/geobrix/test/pyrx/test_docs_examples.py"
/>

| SQL (session) | `gbx_rst_fillnodata(tile, [max_search_dist], [smoothing_iter])` | `TILE` |
| Python | `rasterx.functions.rst_fillnodata(tile, max_search_dist=None, smoothing_iter=None, virtualize_dir=None, virtualize_prefix=None, materialize=None)` | Column |

| Parameter | SQL type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `tile` | `TILE` |  | The tile to fill. |
| `max_search_dist` | `DOUBLE` | `100.0` | How far, in pixels, to search for valid pixels to interpolate from. |
| `smoothing_iter` | `INT` | `0` | The number of 3x3 smoothing passes over the filled pixels. |
{/* END GENERATED gbx_rst_fillnodata */}

Powered by **rasterio** (`rasterio.fill`).

The lightweight **Python** binding accepts three optional keyword arguments the SQL and heavyweight tiers do not — `virtualize_dir`, `virtualize_prefix`, and `materialize` — controlling whether the produced tile carries raster bytes or a bytes-free virtual reference. See [Virtual-tile force-output params](#virtual-tile-overrides).
:::

Fill NoData pixels via `gdal.FillNodata` using inverse-distance interpolation from neighbors within `maxSearchDist` pixels. `smoothingIter` applies an optional post-fill 3×3 smoothing pass.

Compute per-band histograms via `band.GetHistogram`. Returns `MAP<STRING, ARRAY<LONG>>` keyed by `"band_<n>"` with bucket counts. If `bands` is null, all bands are processed; if `min` / `max` are null, GDAL auto-detects the range.

_Examples use the multiband fixture (`rgb_nir_small.tif`, 3 bands) so the histogram has entries for each band._

### rst_sample

{/* BEGIN GENERATED gbx_rst_sample: edit manifest/functions.json, then run gbx:manifest:gen */}
<Surfaces session python scala />

| Surface | Call | Returns |
|---|---|---|
| SQL (session) | `gbx_rst_sample(tile, geom, [crs])` | `array<double>` |
| Python | `rasterx.functions.rst_sample(tile, geom, crs=None)` | Column |

| Parameter | SQL type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `tile` | `TILE` |  | The raster tile. |
| `geom` | `BINARY` or `STRING` |  | A POINT as WKB, EWKB, WKT or EWKT. |
| `crs` | `STRING` | `NULL` | The CRS of a point with no SRID: `EPSG:<code>`, `ESRI:<code>`, WKT or an integer code. The raster CRS when NULL. |
{/* END GENERATED gbx_rst_sample */}

See [interpolation & fill](./grid-raster-tessellation#interpolation-and-fill) to choose among sampling, resampling, and gap-filling.

:::note Lightweight tier (pyrx)
Powered by **rasterio**. Point geometries only; the point is assumed to be in the raster's CRS.
:::

Sample the raster at the geometry's location(s). For a `POINT`, returns `ARRAY<DOUBLE>` of one value per band at the nearest pixel. The point moves from its source CRS to the raster CRS. An EWKB or EWKT SRID wins, then the optional `crs` argument. With neither, the point is taken to be in the raster CRS. See [Coordinate Reference Systems](./coordinate-reference-systems#source-crs-vs-output-crs).

:::tip Esri RS_VALUE / raster sampling
`rst_sample` is GeoBrix's equivalent of the ArcGIS **Sample** tool's `RS_VALUE` — reading a raster's cell value at a point. It returns `ARRAY<DOUBLE>` (one value per band, in band order), or `null` where the point falls outside the raster (the NoData/out-of-extent case). Sampling is nearest-pixel; for bilinear or cubic, run [`rst_resample`](#rst_resample) first, then sample.

- **Multiple rasters** (Esri's `RS_VALUE1`, `RS_VALUE2`, …): call `rst_sample` once per raster and alias each result column.
- **A points table against a raster tile set** (the Sample-tool workflow at scale): join the points to the tiles that contain them — with `st_intersects`, or a raster grid tessellation such as `rst_h3_tessellate` for an index join — then call `rst_sample(tile, point)` per row.

See [Raster Sampling](./raster-sampling) for the full workflow guide.
:::

### rst_setcrs

{/* BEGIN GENERATED gbx_rst_setcrs: edit manifest/functions.json, then run gbx:manifest:gen */}
<Surfaces session python scala />

| Surface | Call | Returns |
|---|---|---|
| SQL (session) | `gbx_rst_setcrs(tile, crs)` | `TILE` |
| Python | `rasterx.functions.rst_setcrs(tile, crs)` | Column |

| Parameter | SQL type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `tile` | `TILE` |  | The tile to relabel. |
| `crs` | `STRING` |  | The CRS to stamp: an authority code such as `'EPSG:3857'` or `'ESRI:54008'`, WKT, PROJ4, PROJJSON, or an integer string (an EPSG code). A value that is no CRS raises. |
{/* END GENERATED gbx_rst_setcrs */}

:::note Lightweight tier (pyrx)
Powered by **rasterio** + **pyproj**. Stamps the CRS without reprojecting.
:::

Stamp a CRS onto a raster that lacks a spatial reference, or has a wrong one, from a CRS *string* (`EPSG:x` / `ESRI:x` / WKT / PROJ4). An int-castable string is treated as a SRID. Does NOT reproject — only rewrites the CRS metadata. Use `rst_transformcrs` when you need an actual reprojection. See [Coordinate Reference Systems](./coordinate-reference-systems).

Distinct from `rst_setsrid` (integer EPSG only): `rst_setcrs` accepts any CRS string — an authority code (`EPSG:3857`, `ESRI:54008`), WKT, or PROJ4 — so a non-EPSG CRS can be applied. An int-castable string (`'4326'`) behaves like `rst_setsrid(tile, 4326)`. See the [CRS: SRID int vs CRS string](#rst_crs) note above.

### rst_setsrid

{/* BEGIN GENERATED gbx_rst_setsrid: edit manifest/functions.json, then run gbx:manifest:gen */}
<Surfaces session python scala />

| Surface | Call | Returns |
|---|---|---|
| SQL (session) | `gbx_rst_setsrid(tile, srid)` | `TILE` |
| Python | `rasterx.functions.rst_setsrid(tile, srid, virtualize_dir=None, virtualize_prefix=None, materialize=None)` | Column |

| Parameter | SQL type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `tile` | `TILE` |  | The tile to relabel. |
| `srid` | `INT` |  | The code to stamp, an EPSG or ESRI code; `0` clears the CRS. A negative number, or a positive code that is neither, raises. |
{/* END GENERATED gbx_rst_setsrid */}

:::note Lightweight tier (pyrx)
Powered by **rasterio**. Stamps the CRS without reprojecting.
:::

:::note Lightweight Python — virtual-tile force-output
The lightweight **Python** binding accepts three optional keyword arguments the SQL and heavyweight tiers do not — `virtualize_dir`, `virtualize_prefix`, and `materialize` — controlling whether the produced tile carries raster bytes or a bytes-free virtual reference. See [Virtual-tile force-output params](#virtual-tile-overrides).

Stamp a SRID (`>= 0`; an EPSG or ESRI code) onto a raster that lacks (or has a wrong) spatial reference; `0` clears the CRS. Does NOT reproject — only rewrites the CRS metadata. A negative SRID is rejected, and a positive code that is neither EPSG nor ESRI raises when stamped. Use `rst_transform` when you need an actual reprojection, or `rst_setcrs` to stamp from a CRS string. See [Coordinate Reference Systems](./coordinate-reference-systems).

Keep the pixels for which `pixel op value` holds, with their original values, and set the others to NoData. `op` is one of `>`, `>=`, `<`, `<=`, `==`, `!=`. A source without NoData gets the data type's fallback value, such as 65535 for `UInt16`.

## Analysis

Higher-level analytical transforms wrapping single GDAL primitives — COG layout publishing, proximity surfaces, contour extraction, and viewshed analysis. See the [Helios notebooks](../notebooks/helios) for a worked example of `gbx_rst_cog_convert` converting 3DEP DEMs to COGs and cataloging them in a STAC Delta table (NB03).

### rst_cog_convert

{/* BEGIN GENERATED gbx_rst_cog_convert: edit manifest/functions.json, then run gbx:manifest:gen */}
<Surfaces session python scala />

| Surface | Call | Returns |
|---|---|---|
| SQL (session) | `gbx_rst_cog_convert(tile, [compression], [blocksize], [overview_resampling])` | `TILE` |
| Python | `rasterx.functions.rst_cog_convert(tile, compression=None, blocksize=None, overview_resampling=None, virtualize_dir=None, virtualize_prefix=None, materialize=None)` | Column |

| Parameter | SQL type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `tile` | `TILE` |  | The tile to convert. |
| `compression` | `STRING` | `'AUTO'` | `'AUTO'` (a size-adaptive ZSTD with the predictor for the data type) or a codec: `ZSTD`, `DEFLATE`, `LZW`, `LERC`, `JPEG`, `WEBP` or `NONE`. Any other word raises. |
| `blocksize` | `INT` | `512` | The internal block size in pixels, square: a multiple of 16. Any other value raises. |
| `overview_resampling` | `STRING` | `'AVERAGE'` | The resampling of the overview pyramid, such as `AVERAGE`. |
{/* END GENERATED gbx_rst_cog_convert */}

:::note Lightweight tier (pyrx)
Re-encodes the tile as a Cloud Optimized GeoTIFF (it passes GDAL's COG validator). `compression` defaults to `"auto"` — a **size-adaptive ZSTD** level with a dtype-matched predictor (the GeoBrix materialize baseline; see [Materialized Compression](./materialized-compression)) — or an explicit codec name (`zstd`, `deflate`, `lzw`, `lerc`, `jpeg`, `webp`, `none`). The tile's `metadata.driver` is `GTiff` (a COG is a valid GeoTIFF).
:::

:::note Lightweight Python — virtual-tile force-output
The lightweight **Python** binding accepts three optional keyword arguments the SQL and heavyweight tiers do not — `virtualize_dir`, `virtualize_prefix`, and `materialize` — controlling whether the produced tile carries raster bytes or a bytes-free virtual reference. See [Virtual-tile force-output params](#virtual-tile-overrides).
:::

Re-layout a raster tile as a Cloud Optimized GeoTIFF via `gdal.Translate -of COG`. Both tiers use a **ZSTD + dtype-predictor** baseline. `compression` accepts an explicit codec on either tier (`ZSTD`, `DEFLATE`, `LZW`, `NONE`, `LERC`, `JPEG`, `WEBP`) and `AUTO`, its default. `AUTO` is a size-adaptive ZSTD level with the dtype predictor on the lightweight tier and ZSTD on the heavyweight tier. `blocksize` is the internal tile size in pixels (square), any multiple of 16 down to 16, on both tiers. `overviewResampling` is the algorithm for the auto-generated overview pyramid. Output is a GTiff-on-disk variant suitable for HTTP range serving. See [Materialized Compression](./materialized-compression) for the codec/level details and when to override.

  scala={scalaApiExamplesCode}          scalaSource="docs/tests/scala/api/ScalaApiExamples.scala"
  testFile="docs/tests/python/api/test_rasterx_tileops_python_light.py"

### rst_contour

{/* BEGIN GENERATED gbx_rst_contour: edit manifest/functions.json, then run gbx:manifest:gen */}
<Surfaces session python scala />

| Surface | Call | Returns |
|---|---|---|
| SQL (session) | `gbx_rst_contour(tile, levels, [interval], [base], [attr_field])` | `array<struct<geom_wkb:binary,value:double>>` |
| Python | `rasterx.functions.rst_contour(tile, levels, interval=None, base=None, attr_field=None)` | Column |

| Parameter | SQL type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `tile` | `TILE` |  | The tile to trace. |
| `levels` | `ARRAY<DOUBLE>` |  | The values to trace; NULL or empty to use `interval` and `base`. |
| `interval` | `DOUBLE` | `0.0` | The spacing of the lines, used when `levels` is empty; above 0. |
| `base` | `DOUBLE` | `0.0` | The value one line is drawn at, used with `interval`. |
| `attr_field` | `STRING` | `'elev'` | Accepted for compatibility; the value is always the struct field `value`. |
{/* END GENERATED gbx_rst_contour */}

:::note Lightweight tier (pyrx)
Powered by **scikit-image** (`measure.find_contours`). Returns `ARRAY<struct(geom_wkb, value)>` of contour LineStrings (in the raster CRS) at each fixed `level`, or at `base + k*interval` across the data range when `levels` is empty; NoData is masked before tracing. The marching-squares line geometry differs slightly from the heavyweight GDAL contours.
:::

Generate contour LineString features via `gdal.ContourGenerateEx`. Pass a non-empty `levels` `ARRAY<DOUBLE>` for fixed contour values, or pass `array()` and set `interval` (>0) for equal-step contours at `base + n*interval`. Returns `ARRAY<struct(geom_wkb BINARY, value DOUBLE)>` — one entry per contour line in the source raster's CRS.

### rst_proximity

{/* BEGIN GENERATED gbx_rst_proximity: edit manifest/functions.json, then run gbx:manifest:gen */}
<Surfaces session python scala />

| Surface | Call | Returns |
|---|---|---|
| SQL (session) | `gbx_rst_proximity(tile, [target_values], [dist_units], [max_distance])` | `TILE` |
| Python | `rasterx.functions.rst_proximity(tile, target_values=None, dist_units=None, max_distance=None, virtualize_dir=None, virtualize_prefix=None, materialize=None)` | Column |

| Parameter | SQL type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `tile` | `TILE` |  | The tile to measure: its first band. |
| `target_values` | `STRING` | `NULL` | The source pixel values as a comma-separated list, such as `'1,2'`. NULL makes every pixel that is not 0 a source. |
| `dist_units` | `STRING` | `'GEO'` | `'GEO'` measures in the units of the tile's CRS; `'PIXEL'` counts pixels. NULL is `'GEO'`. |
| `max_distance` | `DOUBLE` | `NULL` | The largest distance to compute, above 0: farther pixels are NoData. NULL for no limit. |
{/* END GENERATED gbx_rst_proximity */}

:::note Lightweight tier (pyrx)
Powered by **SciPy** (`scipy.ndimage.distance_transform_edt`). Distance to the nearest source pixel (`target_values`, or any non-zero pixel by default), in `GEO` (CRS units) or `PIXEL` units; pixels beyond `max_distance` → NoData −1.0. Single-band Float32.
:::

:::note Lightweight Python — virtual-tile force-output
The lightweight **Python** binding accepts three optional keyword arguments the SQL and heavyweight tiers do not — `virtualize_dir`, `virtualize_prefix`, and `materialize` — controlling whether the produced tile carries raster bytes or a bytes-free virtual reference. See [Virtual-tile force-output params](#virtual-tile-overrides).
:::

Compute a Float32 raster where each pixel holds the distance to the nearest source pixel via `gdal.ComputeProximity`. `targetValues` is a comma-separated list of source-pixel values (e.g. `"1,2,3"`); `null` means every pixel that is not 0 is a source. `distUnits` is `"GEO"` (CRS ground units, default; NULL is `"GEO"`) or `"PIXEL"`; any other word raises an error. `maxDistance` caps the output; pixels beyond it get the NoData sentinel `-1.0`.

### rst_viewshed

{/* BEGIN GENERATED gbx_rst_viewshed: edit manifest/functions.json, then run gbx:manifest:gen */}
<Surfaces session python />

| Surface | Call | Returns |
|---|---|---|
| SQL (session) | `gbx_rst_viewshed(tile, observer_geom, observer_height, [target_height], [max_distance], [crs])` | `TILE` |
| Python | `rasterx.functions.rst_viewshed(tile, observer_geom, observer_height, target_height=None, max_distance=None, crs=None, virtualize_dir=None, virtualize_prefix=None, materialize=None)` | Column |

| Parameter | SQL type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `tile` | `TILE` |  | The DEM tile. |
| `observer_geom` | `BINARY` or `STRING` |  | The observer: a POINT as WKB, EWKB, WKT or EWKT, in the DEM's CRS (an SRID in EWKB or EWKT wins over `crs`). Any other geometry raises. |
| `observer_height` | `DOUBLE` |  | The observer's height above the DEM, 0 or more. |
| `target_height` | `DOUBLE` | `1.6` | The height of the target above the DEM at each cell, 0 or more. |
| `max_distance` | `DOUBLE` | `NULL` | The largest distance to analyse in the DEM's CRS units, above 0. NULL for no limit: the whole DEM. A limit gives the window around the observer. |
| `crs` | `STRING` | `NULL` | The CRS of the observer when it has no SRID (`EPSG:n`, `ESRI:n` or WKT). NULL means the DEM's CRS. |
{/* END GENERATED gbx_rst_viewshed */}

:::note Lightweight tier (pyrx)
Runs GDAL's viewshed (`GDALViewshedGenerate`) with the same settings as the heavyweight tier. Returns a Byte tile (255 visible / 0 invisible) from a DEM tile and a POINT observer.
:::

:::info Changed in 0.6.0
With a `maxDistance`, the light tier now returns the window around the observer (aligned to the DEM's pixel grid), as the heavyweight tier always did; the v0.5.2 light tier stretched that window over the whole DEM on DEMs above its small-DEM limit (about 366 × 366 pixels by default).
:::

:::note Lightweight Python — virtual-tile force-output
The lightweight **Python** binding accepts three optional keyword arguments the SQL and heavyweight tiers do not — `virtualize_dir`, `virtualize_prefix`, and `materialize` — controlling whether the produced tile carries raster bytes or a bytes-free virtual reference. See [Virtual-tile force-output params](#virtual-tile-overrides).
:::

Compute a binary viewshed Byte raster (`255` = visible, `0` = invisible / out-of-range) from a DEM tile and an observer POINT via `gdal.ViewshedGenerate`. `observerGeom` is a WKB / WKT POINT. It is reprojected from its source CRS to the raster CRS. An EWKB/EWKT embedded SRID wins, else the optional `crs` argument, else the point is assumed already aligned. Non-POINT geometries raise an error at execution time. Heights are above the DEM at each pixel. `maxDistance` clips the search radius; `null` = unlimited. See [Coordinate Reference Systems](./coordinate-reference-systems#source-crs-vs-output-crs).

<FunctionExamples
  name="rst_viewshed"
  sql={rasterxSqlCode}                  sqlSource="docs/tests/python/api/rasterx_functions_sql.py"
  python={rasterxTerrainLightCode} pythonSource="docs/tests/python/api/rasterx_terrain_python_light.py"
  testFile="docs/tests/python/api/test_rasterx_terrain_python_light.py"
